# Preface

---

By the grace of the Supreme Absolute Being, for the benefit of the people of this sea-girt world, the great Sage Agastya—the supreme teacher and jewel of the wisdom of the Vedas who resides on the Pothigai Hills—conceived in his spirit a meditation on the Five Sacred Letters. To ensure that all might understand this as a form of astrology, he presented it through the symbolism of "birds" and their behaviors. This science was established so that those practicing the four paths of worship—Devotion, Ritual, Yoga, and Wisdom <note>original: 'சரிதை கிரியை யோகம் ஞானம்' (Carya, Kriya, Yoga, Jnana); the four stages of spiritual progress in Saiva Siddhanta</note>—as well as people of all four social classes, might attain the empire of liberation and consciousness. It is intended to guide each person according to their own spiritual practice toward the four states of salvation: residing in the divine realm, being near the Divine, attaining the divine form, and union with the Divine. <note>original: 'சாலோக சாமீப சாரூப சாயுச்சிய' (Saloka, Samipa, Sarupa, Sayujya); the progressive stages of liberation in Hindu theology.</note>

From the foundational texts of the **Science of the Five Birds**, the following have been compiled and printed:

1.  **First:** The various types of bird science.
2.  **Second:** The daytime classifications for the waxing moon.
3.  **Third:** The nighttime classifications for the waxing moon.
4.  **Fourth:** The daytime classifications for the waning moon.
5.  **Fifth:** The nighttime classifications for the waning moon.
6.  **Sixth:** The results and effects of their specific actions.
7.  **Seventh:** The practical benefits of the birds.
8.  **Eighth:** The interpretation of omens and signs.
9.  **Ninth:** The applications of mathematical grammar.
10. **Tenth:** The results and applications of spiritual attainment.

These subjects were originally expressed subtly. However, for the sake of clarity, Manickathampiran—the court poet of the Madurai Chidambara Desikar Monastery residing in Kancheepuram—has gathered supporting evidence from the *Chudamani Astrology*, the *Kerala Shastra*, the twelfth section of the *Mandalapurudar Dictionary*, commentaries on prosody, and various other ancient works. He has woven these together into this comprehensive collection of the **Science of the Five Birds**, including the relevant mystical diagrams. Additionally, the **Original Text and Commentary on the Wisdom of Breath**, which Lord Shiva taught to the Goddess Uma Maheshwari, has been included in this printing.

Though there may be inconsistencies with the strict rules of literature and grammar—such as errors in letters, syllables, feet, connections, lines, rhymes, or the nuances of poetic ornament—we ask that you view this as a work of the ancient masters. Just as the learned accept the wisdom of the elders without dwelling on flaws, we request you to forgive any slips of the hand. If students who possess the complete original manuscripts assist in refining this work, it will be a great service to the world.

->The Sacred Space of Consciousness.<- <note>original: 'திருச்சிற்றம்பலம்' (Thiruchitrambalam); a sacred exclamation referring to the Chidambaram temple, signifying the heart or the space of pure consciousness.</note>

<summary>This preface attributes the Five Birds astrological system to Sage Agastya and outlines ten chapters covering lunar phases, omens, and spiritual practice. It also acknowledges the scholarly compilation work of Manickathampiran, who integrated various classical sources and the "Wisdom of Breath" (Jnana Sara Nool) into this edition.</summary>

# The Science of the Five Birds.
<note>original: 'பஞ்சபட்சிசாஸ்திரம்' (Panchapakshi Shastra). This is a unique system of Tamil astrology that categorizes human nature and timing into five symbolic birds: the Hawk, the Owl, the Crow, the Rooster, and the Peacock.</note>

---
->Verses in the *Nerisai-Venpa* Meter.<- <note>A classical Tamil poetic form known for its strict rhythmic rules.</note>

By contemplating the first letter uttered by a person ........... *
By visualizing it as a specific bird—
To speak the truth of the bird governing that direction ........... *
The essence of the arts and epics is our protection .................... (1)

O Lady who dwells in the honey-dripping flower, ........... *
I have worshipped your golden, lotus-like feet—
That the nature of the five types of birds in this world ........... *
May reside firmly within my mind, please grant... .................... (2)
<note>The "Lady in the flower" refers to Saraswati, the Goddess of Wisdom and Arts.</note>

The bird of the direction, the messenger who arrives, ........... *
The physical stance, the rotation of time—
You have spoken well and without error... ........... *
The method of the complete collection as told by the experts... .................... (3)

### Chapter One.
<note>original: 'முதலாவததிகாரம்' (Muthalavathikaram)</note>

The first letter of the speech arises first; ........... *
Observe also the physical state of the speaker—
Identify and understand the variations coming from the name... ........... *
Research and then speak the knowledge... .................... (4)

If a person asks about the good or bad outcomes regarding a specific task, one should analyze the first letter of their statement, the posture they are in when asking, and the specific nuances of their name to determine and speak the omen.

**Panchapakshi Shastra**: The Science of the Five Birds (a system of divination).
**Muthalavathikaram**: The First Chapter.
**Nerisai-Venpa**: A specific four-line poetic meter in Tamil literature.
</vocab>

# The Science of the Five Birds.

->The Letters Assigned to the Birds.<-

The letter **'A'** is indeed the Hawk; the Owl is the letter **'I'** ........... *
The letter **'U'** is the Crow of old—in the letter [E?] ........... *
The letter **'E'** is the Rooster, and at all times the Peacock ....... *
The letter **'O'** is declared by the consonant-vowel ........... *
<note>This verse establishes the "vowel-bird" correspondence which is the core of this divination system. The gaps (........ *) indicate where the original manuscript or OCR is fragmentary.</note>

| Letter | Bird Symbol |
| :--- | :--- |
| **அ** (A) | **Hawk** (*Valluru*) |
| **இ** (I) | **Owl** (*Anthai*) |
| **உ** (U) | **Crow** (*Kakam*) |
| **எ** (E) | **Rooster** (*Kozhi*) |
| **ஒ** (O) | **Peacock** (*Mayil*) |

<note>The image described in the source depicts these five birds arranged in a sacred grid, each representing different temperaments and planetary influences.</note>

Furthermore, it should be understood that these [birds] are also categorized by their **consonant-vowels**. <note>original: 'உயிர்மெய்' (uyirmey); in Tamil grammar, this refers to the combination of a consonant and a vowel. This implies that names starting with 'Ka', 'Ki', 'Ku', etc., follow the bird assigned to the vowel 'a', 'i', or 'u'.</note>

The term **Hawk** (*Valluru*) is used to refer to both the hawk and the kite. <note>original: 'குருமான்' (kuruman); likely referring to a specific species of raptor or kite common in the region.</note>

### Vocabulary
*   **Hawk** (*Valluru*): The first bird, symbolizing strength and swift action.
*   **Owl** (*Anthai*): The second bird, often associated with hidden wisdom or nighttime activity.
*   **Crow** (*Kakam*): The third bird, representing social intelligence and persistence.
*   **Rooster/Fowl** (*Kozhi*): The fourth bird, symbolizing alertness and the dawn.
*   **Peacock** (*Mayil/Mannai*): The fifth bird, representing beauty, pride, and completion.
*   **Letters** (*Atcharangal*): <note>original: 'அட்சரங்கள்' (aksharas); refers to the sacred sounds or alphabets used to determine one's bird.</note>

<summary>This page defines the primary correspondence between the five Tamil short vowels (A, I, U, E, O) and the five symbolic birds used in this astrological system. It also clarifies that these rules extend to consonant-vowel combinations and provides a broad definition for the 'Hawk' category.</summary>
<keywords>Panchapakshi, Tamil Astrology, Vowels, Hawk, Owl, Crow, Rooster, Peacock, Divination</keywords>

<meta>Continuity: On the previous page, the text introduced the five symbolic birds—the Hawk, Owl, Crow, Rooster, and Peacock—and assigned them to the five primary Tamil vowels. This page now details the "Five Activities" (Panchavasthas) of these birds and the complex mathematical divisions of time used to calculate their influence over human destiny.</meta>

# The Science of the Five Birds.

->For both Lunar Fortnights.<-
<note>original: 'பக்ஷம்' (Paksham); refers to the two halves of the lunar month: the waxing and waning periods.</note>
->A Note on the Activities of the Birds.<-
->Six-foot Long Verse in the Asiriya Meter.<-

Eating, walking, sleeping, dying, and kingly rule are found in the high dreams... *
On this wide earth, they rule as kings, then sleep, then walk in the proper way and rest... *
In the waning fortnight, the day begins like this, with sleeping and then ruling... *
In the night, there is eating, then longing, then going, and finally ruling as king.
<note>This verse introduces the five states of the birds: Eating, Walking, Ruling (as King), Sleeping, and Dying. The "dream" or "vision" likely refers to the divinatory state of the practitioner. The sequence of these activities changes depending on whether the moon is waxing or waning.</note>

->For the Waxing and Waning Fortnights—Fifteen Lunar Days each.<-
<note>original: 'பூர்வபட்சம்' (Purva Paksha - Waxing) and 'அபரபட்சம்' (Apara Paksha - Waning). A 'Tithi' is a lunar day.</note>
->For each Lunar Day, there are ten Watches; for each Watch, there are five units of time.<-
<note>original: 'சாமம்' (Samam/Yamam) refers to a watch of roughly 3 hours; 'நாழிகை' (Nazhigai) is a unit of 24 minutes. The math here is traditionally adjusted based on the bird system.</note>
->Verse in the Nerisai Venba Meter.<-

One and a quarter, one and a half, and one and three-quarters ....... *
Properly, in this way, these units of time—without decrease ........... *
For the waxing moon and the waning moon, the secret variations ........... *
The ancestors of old have established and divided these cycles. (7)

->Six units of time constitute one Watch.<-
<note>original: 'நாழிகை - ஆறு' (Nazhigai - 6). While the header above mentions five, the systematic prose below clarifies that 6 Nazhigai make up one Samam (Watch).</note>
->The Section on Activities.<-

In another way, you must investigate the basic rules ........... *
When one calculates the joining of these combined parts ..... *
Eating, walking, ruling, sleeping, and dying—these will occur ........... *
O girl with eyes like the beetle! Consider this well ... ... ... ... ... ... ... (8)
<note>The poet addresses a "girl with beetle-like eyes," a traditional literary device in Tamil scientific or philosophical texts to make the teaching feel like a personal transmission of wisdom.</note>

->The Section on Functions.<-

For the one who eats, one and a quarter, then one and a half, then one ....... *
In the eyes of the King, we shall see two—with grace .... *
Sleeping is three-quarters, and dying is half a unit ... ... *
This is the result given by the bird that possesses these traits ... ... ... ... ... ... ... (9)

(Among these:) In order to understand the benefit of the three verses of the ancestors, consider the fifteen lunar days of the **waxing moon** <term>Purva Paksha; the period from the new moon to the full moon</term> and the fifteen lunar days of the **waning moon** <term>Apara Paksha; the period from the full moon to the new moon</term>. For each lunar day, there are five **day-watches** and five **night-watches** <note>Totaling 10 watches per 24-hour period.</note>. One watch consists of six **time-units** <term>Nazhigai; a traditional unit where 1 Nazhigai equals 24 minutes; therefore, one watch is 144 minutes or 2 hours and 24 minutes</term>. Among these, the rising bird follows this order: in the first watch, it **eats**; in the second, it **walks**; in the third, it **rules as king**; in the fourth, it **sleeps**; and in the fifth, it **dies**. 

Furthermore, when looking at the **inner influences** <term>Antara Bhukti; a sub-division of time where the bird's primary activity is further influenced by the states of other birds</term>, the duration for each state is as follows:
*   For the eating bird: **1 ¼** units
*   For the walking bird: **1 ½** units
*   For the kingly bird: **2** units
*   For the sleeping bird: **¾** units
*   For the dying bird: **½** units
In this manner, observe the total of **6 units** that make up one watch. (1)

<summary>This page defines the "Five Activities" (Panchavasthas) of the birds—Eating, Walking, Ruling, Sleeping, and Dying—and explains the mathematical division of a lunar day into watches and units (Nazhigai) to determine when these states occur.</summary>
<keywords>Panchavasthas, Five Activities, Tithi, Waxing Moon, Waning Moon, Nazhigai, Samam, Time calculation, Divination states</keywords>

<meta>Continuity: Building on the previous page's introduction of the five symbolic birds and their vowel assignments, this page presents the "Subtle Wheel" (Suksma Chakram). This chart acts as a master key for calculating the specific activities of each bird—and thus the fate of the individual—based on the time of day and the phase of the moon. It also introduces the classification of lunar days (Tithis) and zodiac signs (Rasis), which are essential variables in these astrological calculations.</meta>

# The Science of the Five Birds

To understand the practical application of the verses from the beginning of this book through verse nine:
->The Subtle Wheel<-
<note>original: 'சூட்சுமச்சக்கரம்' (Suksma Chakram). A 'Subtle Wheel' is a condensed mnemonic chart used by practitioners to quickly determine the 'state' or 'activity' of a bird at any given moment.</note>

| Waxing Fortnight | | Waning Fortnight | |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Day** | **Night** | **Day** | **Night** |
| **A - Hawk** | **U - Crow** | **O - Peacock** | **E - Rooster** |
| Activity - Eating | Activity - Eating | Activity - Eating | Activity - Eating |
| **I - Owl** | **I - Owl** | **U - Crow** | **U - Crow** |
| Activity - Walking | Activity - Rule | Activity - Death | Activity - Sleep |
| **U - Crow** | **A - Hawk** | **A - Hawk** | **I - Owl** |
| Activity - Rule | Activity - Death | Activity - Sleep | Activity - Walking |
| **E - Rooster** | **O - Peacock** | **E - Rooster** | **A - Hawk** |
| Activity - Sleep | Activity - Walking | Activity - Rule | Activity - Death |
| **O - Peacock** | **E - Rooster** | **I - Owl** | **O - Peacock** |
| Activity - Death | Activity - Sleep | Activity - Walking | Activity - Rule |

<note>The table shows how the five birds rotate through five activities: Eating (Unndi), Walking (Nadai), Ruling (Arasu), Sleeping (Thookkam), and Death (Maranam). The vowel associated with each bird changes its activity depending on whether it is the Waxing phase (Purva Paksha) or Waning phase (Apara Paksha), and whether it is Day or Night.</note>

In accordance with these—the astrological poet named Chudamani Ullamudaiyan:
->Verse of Eight-foot Long Asiriya Meter<-

->The Divisions of Lunar Days beginning with Nanda<-

If you ask, the first, sixth, and eleventh are *Nanda* [Delight] ........... *
The next, second, seventh, and twelfth, are clearly known as *Bhadra* [Auspicious] ....... *
The pleasant third, eighth, and thirteenth are *Jaya* [Victorious] ....... *
The *Rikta* [Empty] days are the ninth, fourth, and fourteenth ..... *
Joined together, the fifth, the tenth, and the Full/New Moon days ........... *
Are known as *Purna* [Full]; Cancer, Leo, and Capricorn are Moveable signs ........... *
The unique Taurus, Scorpio, Aquarius, and Leo are Fixed signs ........... *
Sagittarius, Gemini, Virgo, and Pisces are known as Dual signs ... ... ... *

<note>This verse explains the grouping of the 15 lunar days (Tithis) into five categories: Nanda, Bhadra, Jaya, Rikta, and Purna. These categories determine the 'strength' of the day. It also classifies the twelve Zodiac signs (Rasis) into three qualities: Moveable (Chara), Fixed (Sthira), and Dual (Dvisvabhava), which are used to further refine the bird's influence on a person born under those signs.</note>

<term>Suksma Chakram</term> — A "subtle" or "hidden" chart used for quick reference in divination.
<term>Purva Paksha</term> — The waxing half of the lunar month (Bright Fortnight).
<term>Apara Paksha</term> — The waning half of the lunar month (Dark Fortnight).
<term>Nanda, Bhadra, Jaya, Rikta, Purna</term> — The five classifications of lunar days (Tithis), representing different levels of prosperity and success.

<meta>Continuity: Following the introduction of the 'Subtle Wheel' which outlines the rhythmic cycles of the five birds (Hawk, Owl, Crow, Rooster, and Peacock), this page provides the foundational astronomical data needed to use that wheel. # The Science of the Five Birds

The first, sixth, and eleventh lunar days are called **The Joyous** <note>original: 'நந்தை' (Nandai)</note>.
The second, seventh, and twelfth lunar days are called **The Prosperous** <note>original: 'பத்திரை' (Bhadrai)</note>.
The third, eighth, and thirteenth lunar days are called **The Assembly** <note>original: 'சபை' (Sabhai)</note>.
The fourth, ninth, and fourteenth lunar days are called **The Empty** <note>original: 'இருத்தை' (Riktai)</note>.
The fifth, tenth, and the New Moon or Full Moon days are called **The Full** <note>original: 'பூரணை' (Purnai)</note>.

Furthermore:
Aries, Cancer, Libra, and Capricorn—these four are **Movable Signs** <note>original: 'சரராசிகள்' (Chara Rasis)</note>.
Taurus, Scorpio, Aquarius, and Leo—these four are **Fixed Signs** <note>original: 'ஸ்திரராசிகள்' (Sthira Rasis)</note>.
Sagittarius, Gemini, Virgo, and Pisces—these four are **Dual Signs** <note>original: 'உபயராசிகள்' (Ubhaya Rasis)</note>.

->The Wheel of Lunar Day Conjunctions<-
<note>This table organizes the 15 days of the lunar fortnight into five groups. Each group is governed by a different quality, which in turn determines the strength of the Five Birds.</note>

| Lunar Component | Part 1 | Part 2 | Part 3 | Part 4 | Part 5 |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Category** | **Joyous** | **Prosperous** | **Assembly** | **Empty** | **Full** |
| **1st Cycle** | 1st Day | 2nd Day | 3rd Day | 4th Day | 5th Day |
| | * | * | * | * | * |
| **2nd Cycle** | 6th Day | 7th Day | 8th Day | 9th Day | 10th Day |
| | * | * | * | * | * |
| **3rd Cycle** | 11th Day | 12th Day | 13th Day | 14th Day | New/Full Moon |
| | * | * | * | * | * |

The Wheel of Zodiac Conjunctions of the Three Classifications
<note>In the Science of the Five Birds, the practitioner's zodiac sign must be classified as Movable, Fixed, or Dual to determine the auspiciousness of an action.</note>

| Movable Signs | Fixed Signs | Dual Signs |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Aries | Taurus | Gemini |
| Cancer | Leo | Virgo |
| Libra | Scorpio | Sagittarius |
| Capricorn | Aquarius | Pisces |

<meta>Continuity: Following the classification of lunar days and zodiac signs, this page introduces the relationship between these lunar cycles, the days of the week, and the five primary vowels (A, I, U, E, and O). # The Science of the Five Birds

<page-num>7</page-num>

->The Conjunction of Vowels for the Five Lunar Phases and Seven Days<-

In the Joyous days of Sunday and Tuesday, the letter 'A' prevails;
For the Prosperous days of Monday and Wednesday, the letter 'I' is set;
In the Assembly days, the golden Jupiter holds the letter 'U' as its own;
The Empty days of Venus take 'E', while for Saturn’s Full days, 'O' is the root;
We declare these divisions of time for the daylight hours.

(Within this:) During the lunar phase of the Joyous <note>original: 'நந்தை' (Nandai)</note> days, on Sundays and Tuesdays, the letter 'A' <note>original: 'அகரம்' (Akara)</note> is the active vowel for the first activity. On the Prosperous <note>original: 'பத்தினி' (Pathini/Bhadrai)</note> days, on Mondays and Wednesdays, the letter 'I' <note>original: 'இகரம்' (Ikara)</note> is active. On the Assembly <note>original: 'சைய' (Saiya/Jaya)</note> days, on Thursdays <note>original: 'குருவாரம்' (Guruvaram)</note>, the letter 'U' <note>original: 'உகரம்' (Ukara)</note> is active. On the Empty <note>original: 'இருத்தை' (Iruthai/Riktai)</note> days, on Fridays, the letter 'E' <note>original: 'எகரம்' (Ekara)</note> is active. On the Full <note>original: 'பூரணை' (Purnai)</note> days, on Saturdays, the letter 'O' <note>original: 'ஒகரம்' (Okara)</note> is active. Furthermore, we have stated these to define the thirty *nazhikai* <term>nazhikai: a traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes; thirty nazhikai constitute the twelve hours from sunrise to sunset</term> from sunrise until sunset. (11)

->The Wheel of Vowel Conjunctions<-

<note>This table shows which vowel initiates the cycle of activities for the Five Birds based on the lunar phase and the day of the week. Each vowel corresponds to one of the five activities: Eating, Walking, Ruling, Sleeping, or Death.</note>

| Day & Phase | Eating | Walking | Ruling | Sleeping | Death |
| :--- | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| \* | \* | | \* | வ | \* |
| **Joyous** (Sun, Tue) | A | I | U | E | O |
| **Prosperous** (Mon, Wed) | I | U | E | O | A |
| **Assembly** (Thu) | U | E | O | A | I |
| **Empty** (Fri) | E | O | A | I | U |
| **Full** (Sat) | O | A | I | U | E |

It provides a table showing how the vowel sequence shifts daily, which is used to calculate the dominant bird's power at any given hour.</summary>

# The Science of the Five Birds

<page-num>7</page-num>

<header>The Science of the Five Birds</header>

->Vowels — The Conjunction of the Five Birds<-

It is said the Hawk eats with 'A', the Owl with 'I', the Crow with 'U' ... \*
The Rooster follows with 'E', and the Peacock consumes with 'O' ... \*
In this ancient order, the birds are counted;
Two for walking, three for ruling, four for sleeping, and five for dying ... [Indeed.

(Within this:) Draw six lines horizontally and six lines vertically to create a square <note>The original text "ஆறுகோடிமுத்து" (six crore pearls/points) likely refers to a grid of points or lines used to draw the 5x5 chart</note>. Divide this into five houses <note>sections or cells</note> for each direction. In the first house, the Hawk—represented by the vowel 'A'—eats. In the second house, the Owl—represented by the vowel 'I'—eats. In the third house, the Crow—represented by the vowel 'U'—eats. In the fourth house, the Rooster—represented by the vowel 'E'—eats. In the fifth house, the Peacock—represented by the vowel 'O'—eats.

Furthermore, following this same order, the bird that eats in the first house will walk in the second house, rule in the third house, sleep in the fourth house, and die in the fifth house. Thus, across these twenty-five houses, the five birds will be situated in their five activities throughout the five *yamams* <term>yamam: a traditional time division; here, the day is divided into five equal segments of approximately 2 hours and 24 minutes each</term> of the day for each day of the week. (12)

->The Wheel of the Conjunction of Birds<-

| Weekdays | Sun & Tue | Mon & Wed | Thu | Fri | Sat |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Lunar Phase** | Joyous <note>original: 'நந்தை' (Nandai)</note> | Prosperous <note>original: 'பத்தினி' (Bhadrai)</note> | Success <note>original: 'சைய' (Jaya)</note> | Empty <note>original: 'இருத்தை' (Riktai)</note> | Full <note>original: 'பூரணை' (Purnai)</note> |
| **1st Time Segment** | Hawk: Eating (A) | Owl: Eating (I) | Crow: Eating (U) | Rooster: Eating (E) | Peacock: Eating (O) |
| **2nd Time Segment** | Walking (A) | Walking (I) | Walking (U) | Walking (E) | Walking (O) |
| **3rd Time Segment** | Ruling (U) | Ruling (U) | Ruling (U) | Ruling (U) | Ruling (U) |
| **4th Time Segment** | Sleeping (O) | Sleeping (O) | Sleeping (O) | Sleeping (O) | Sleeping (O) |
| **5th Time Segment** | Dying (A) | Dying (A) | Dying (A) | Dying (A) | Dying (A) |

<note>The letters A, I, U, E, O in the table represent the Sanskrit/Tamil vowels (Akara, Ikara, etc.) assigned to each activity phase. The table maps out which bird is active based on the day of the week and the specific time of day.</note>

->The Conjunction of Letters and Phrases<-

->Also known as the Conjunction of the Eight Types of Animal Origins<-

->A Verse in the "Six-Foot Long-Lined" Poetic Meter<-

The bent letter 'A' with 'Ka', 'Sa', 'Ta', and 'Na' — \*
The worthy 'Pa' with 'Ma' and 'Va' make these eight letters. \*
From these, the eight studied animal origins <term>yoni: in this context, the symbolic animal "womb" or species associated with a person’s birth</term> arise in order. \*
Through this conjunction, the victory and nature of the birds are declared.

(Within this:) Take these eight letters — A, Ka, Sa, Ta, Na, Pa, Ma, Va <note>original: "அ-க-ச-த-ந-ப-ம-வ"</note> — and assign them to the eight directions, starting from the East. Then, associate them with the eight animal origins: the Eagle <term>Garuda: a divine mythical bird</term>, Cat, Lion, Dog, Snake, Rat, Elephant, and Rabbit. By examining these animal origins and the letters, one can determine the strengths and weaknesses of the birds, their victories or defeats, and their friendships or enmities. This is how it should be understood. (13)

->The Alignment of Alphabetical Potency<-

The first letter is the **Child**, the second is the **Youth**, \*
The third is the **King**, the fourth is the **Elder**, and the fifth is **Death**... \*
By observing the letter of one's name and its spread, \*
Subtract from eight to see the path to victory... \*

(Within this) The five vowels—**A, I, U, E, O**—are placed within the wheel. By observing the letters of one's name in this order: if it matches the first letter, it is called the **Child** <note>Tamil: *Balan*—representing a state of growth but limited power</note>; if the second, the **Youth** <note>Tamil: *Kumaran*—representing peak energy</note>; if the third, the **King** <note>Tamil: *Arasan*—representing total authority and success</note>; if the fourth, the **Elder** <note>Tamil: *Virutthan*—representing declining strength</note>; and if the fifth, it is called **Death** <note>Tamil: *Maranam*—representing a state of total inactivity or failure</note>. 

Furthermore, there is another method for these states (Child, Youth, King, Elder, Death) using the following vowel pairs: **A-Ā, I-Ī, U-Ū, E-Ē, O-Ō**. One must count using the first letter of the subject's name as the starting point. Among these, the first three states bring prosperity, while the latter two bring misfortune. These should not result in the numbers 5 or 6 at the end. By removing the "death-letter" <note>An inauspicious letter based on calculation</note> from my former name, counting the remaining letters, multiplying by **Y** <note>7</note>, and dividing by **A** <note>8</note>, the resulting remainder will reveal which of the two names is more compatible. (5)

->The Compatibility of the Name-Yoni<-
<note>*Yoni* refers to the animal origin or symbolic biological temperament associated with a name.</note>

Count the letters of the name, multiply by seven, and divide by eight... \*
Observe the remainder, counting from the **Garuda** <note>The divine eagle</note> onward... \*
That will be the **Yoni**; those that follow are the enemies... \*
Count from the **Elephant** to find the natural compatibility of the Yonis. (6)

To know the strategy for one person to defeat another... \*
Count the natural letters found in both their names... \*
Multiply that total by ten and divide by eight... \*
The one with the higher remainder will surely see the other person lose. (7)

->The Positions of the Sun (in the Kali Viruttam meter)<-

The positions of the Sun: the **Garuda**, a **Cat**, \*
The fierce **Lion**, and the **Sun** itself becoming... \*
The dark-eyed **Snake**, the **Elephant**, and the **Rabbit**... \*
In whichever place they reside, speak the results accordingly. (8)

### The Waxing Moon Phase <note>original: *Purva Paksha*—the fifteen days between the new moon and the full moon.</note>

Starting with the letter 'A', the five short vowels themselves... ✳ <note>The vowels are A, I, U, E, O.</note>

They perform Eating, Walking, Ruling, Sleeping, and Dying... ✳ <note>These are the five states of activity for each bird.</note>

It is the wondrous Eating, Dying, Walking, Ruling, and Sleeping... ✳
By observing the time when there is no sleep or beautiful death... ✳
Consulting many such verses will bring great benefit... ✳

<page-num>12</page-num>
<header>The Science of the Five Birds</header>

## The Wheel of Time

In the small circle at the center of this wheel are the birds. Depending on which bird is assigned to which day of the week, look from that bird toward the activities listed in the large outer circle—ranging from **Eating** to **Death**. 

If you rotate the wheel to the right, it calculates for the **Waxing Moon Phase** <note>original: *Purva Paksha*—the fifteen days between the new moon and the full moon</note>. This is based on the 15 lunar days <note>original: *Tithi*</note> and the 7 days of the week. To align with the daytime calculations, the activities follow a direct sequence. However, to align with the nighttime calculations, you must skip every other activity (e.g., from **Eating** to **Sleep**, then to **Rule**) as you rotate.

Conversely, if you rotate toward this side (to the left) starting from the end, it calculates for the **Waning Moon Phase** <note>original: *Amara Paksha*—the fifteen days between the full moon and the new moon</note>. For the daytime calculation, it follows the sequence until **Rule**, and for the nighttime calculation, it rotates by skipping every other activity.

<image-desc>
**The Wheel of Time (Kala Chakram)**

A circular astrological diagram used to calculate the potency of the five birds.

*   **Inner Ring (The Five Birds):** Hawk <note>original: *Valluru*</note>, Owl <note>original: *Aanthai*</note>, Crow <note>original: *Kakam*</note>, Rooster <note>original: *Kozhi*</note>, and Peacock <note>original: *Mayil*</note>.
*   **Outer Ring (The Five States):** Death <note>original: *Maranam*</note>, Ruling <note>original: *Arasu*</note>, Sleep <note>original: *Nithirai*</note>, Walking <note>original: *Nadai*</note>, and Eating <note>original: *Undi*</note>.
*   **Directional Petals:** 
    *   Top: **To find the time of Eating**
    *   Bottom: **11 Nazhigai** <note>Approx. 4 hours and 24 minutes</note>
    *   Right: **Waxing Moon Phase**
    *   Left: **Waning Moon Phase**
*   **Numerical Markings:** Various traditional Tamil numerals indicating time units (*Nazhigai*).
</image-desc>

For 1 full day, there are 60 <term>Nazhigai</term> <note>A traditional unit of time; 1 Nazhigai equals 24 minutes</note>, which is equal to 10 <term>Yaamam</term> <note>A "watch" or period of time; here, 1 Yaamam equals 144 minutes</note>. Therefore, 1 *Yaamam* consists of 6 *Nazhigai*. 

Within this system, a daytime period of 30 *Nazhigai* is divided into 5 *Yaamam*, and a nighttime period of 30 *Nazhigai* is likewise divided into 5 *Yaamam*. These should be understood and divided according to the "ruling" periods of the seasons. Rather than becoming confused by reading this entire book, it is far more beneficial to gain clarity by observing the subtle secrets revealed through this wheel.

It simplifies complex calculations into a visual rotation of concentric circles.</summary>

# Chapter Two
---

## Day Verses for the Waxing Moon <note>Tamil: "Purva Paksha," the period of the brightening moon.</note>
### Verse in the Six-Foot Meter <note>A specific Tamil poetic form called "Aruseer-kazhi-nedila-adiya-asiriya-viruttham."</note>

->The Eating Birds for the Seven Days<-

The Hawk is supreme on Tuesday and Sunday; the Owl on Monday and Wednesday;
The Crow is considered on Thursday; the Cock on Friday;
The Peacock is the eating bird on Saturday; after investigating these daytimes,
The Great Sage <note>Refers to Sage Agastya, the traditional author of this system.</note> declared these to the world surrounded by the sea. (1)

->The Dying Birds for the Seven Days<-

On Sunday and Friday, the Hawk meets its death;
On Monday, the Crow meets its certain death;
On Tuesday, the Cock; on Wednesday, the Peacock meets death;
O lady with dark tresses! These are the birds that die during the first fortnight <note>The Waxing Moon phase.</note>. (2)

### Straight Meter Verse — Activities of the Birds <note>Tamil: "Nerisai Venpa."</note>

Eating, Walking, Ruling the world, Sleeping,
And then, as established of old, Death—these occur in the morning.
After Eating, Ruling, and Walking many paths,
Sleep comes in the middle of the night. (3)

->The Division of the Five Watches of the Day for the Seven Days<-
->The Five Letters, Five Birds, and the Results for the Five Watches<- <note>In this system, the day is divided into five "watches" or "yamas" of approximately 2 hours and 24 minutes each.</note>

### Sunday and Tuesday — Day

<margin>1</margin>
The Hawk eats, and the Owl walks;
The Cock sits on the good throne of Ruling;
The Cock <note>The text suggests a transition or specific state here.</note> is the one that sleeps;
Know that it is the Peacock that dies. (4)

### Saturday — Day

<margin>2</margin>
The beautiful Peacock eats, and the fierce Hawk
Walks with great speed—O lady of pure speech!—
The Owl acts as the Ruling King, while the Crow sleeps,
And the beautiful Golden Cock dies. (5)

### Friday — Day

<margin>3</margin>
The Cock eats its food, and the Peacock leaps and walks;
The Hawk becomes the living King; listen, my friend:
The Owl sleeps, and the Crow itself dies;
Consider this as a beautiful guide for mankind. (6)

<summary>This page details the specific behavioral cycles (Eating, Walking, Ruling, Sleeping, Dying) of the five symbolic birds for each day of the week during the Waxing Moon phase. It attributes these astrological rules to Sage Agastya and provides a mnemonic guide for determining auspicious and inauspicious times.</summary>

<keywords>Five Bird Astrology, Pancha Pakshi Shastra, Waxing Moon, Purva Paksha, Agastya, Tamil Astrology, Hawk, Owl, Crow, Cock, Peacock, Weekly cycles</keywords>

# The Science of the Five Birds <note>Tamil: "Panchapakshi Sastram," a system of biorhythmic divination where five birds represent different cosmic energies affecting a person based on their birth star and the current time.</note>

## Thursday—Daytime

<margin>6</margin>
While the Crow eats, the black Cock walks; . . . . . *
The great and mighty Peacock becomes the Ruler—with grace . . . . . *
While the Hawk sleeps, the strong Owl dies; . . . . . . . *
This is the sequence that the spoken word reveals. . . . . . . . . . . . (7)

## Monday and Wednesday—Daytime

<margin>5</margin>
While the Owl eats its meal, the rare Crow walks; . . . . . *
Staying in its place, the Cock becomes the Sovereign—the calm . . . . . *
Peacock sleeps, and the Hawk dies; . . . . . . . . . . . *
See this as the established image. . . . . . . . . . . . (8)

## The Waxing Moon Phase—Daytime <note>Tamil: "Purva Paksha," the fifteen days from the New Moon to the Full Moon.</note>

The calculation for the 15 lunar days <term>Tithi|A lunar day in the Hindu calendar</term> and the 7 days of the week is as follows: The daytime consists of 30 <term>Nazhigai|A traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes</term>, which is divided into 5 <term>Yama|A 'watch' or time period lasting approximately 2 hours and 24 minutes</term>. Therefore, each watch consists of 6 *nazhigai*.

->The Wheel of Mnemonic Sentences<- <note>Tamil: "Vakkiya Chakkaram." These are mnemonic phrases used by practitioners to remember which bird is performing which activity at a given time.</note>

# The Science of the Five Birds <note>original: "Panchapakshi Sastram." A traditional South Indian system of biorhythms used to determine auspicious times based on five birds representing five elemental energies.</note>

->The Daytime Birds for the Seven Days of the Week<-

In the first watch—the bird that rises performs **Eating**. In the 2nd watch, it performs **Walking**. In the 3rd watch, it performs **Ruling**. In the 4th watch, it performs **Sleeping**, and in the 5th watch, it performs **Death**. Furthermore, when examining the internal sub-periods <term>Anthara Buthi|Specific subdivisions of time within a major period, used for more precise timing</term> for a single watch of 6 *nazhigai* <note>A unit of time equal to 24 minutes; 6 nazhigai equals one 'watch' of 2 hours and 24 minutes.</note>: **Eating** lasts 1 ¼; **Walking** lasts 1 ½; **Ruling** lasts 2; **Sleeping** lasts ½; and **Death** lasts ¾. Thus, one should determine these sub-periods for every watch.

->The Vowel-based Systematic Chart<- <note>original: "Ezhuthu Murai Chakkaram." This table uses vowels as shorthand for the five birds: A (அ) for the Vulture, I (இ) for the Owl, U (உ) for the Crow, E (எ) for the Cock, and O (ஒ) for the Peacock.</note>

| Sunday - Tuesday | | | | | | | Saturday | | | | | |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Watch** | **Eating** | **Walking** | **Ruling** | **Sleeping** | **Death** | | **Watch** | **Eating** | **Walking** | **Ruling** | **Sleeping** | **Death** |
| 1 | A | I | U | E | O | | 1 | O | A | I | U | E |
| 2 | O | A | I | U | E | | 2 | E | O | A | I | U |
| 3 | E | O | A | I | U | | 3 | U | E | O | A | I |
| 4 | U | E | O | A | I | | 4 | I | U | E | O | A |
| 5 | I | U | E | O | A | | 5 | A | I | U | E | O |

---

| Friday | | | | | | | Thursday | | | | | |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Watch** | **Eating** | **Walking** | **Ruling** | **Sleeping** | **Death** | | **Watch** | **Eating** | **Walking** | **Ruling** | **Sleeping** | **Death** |
| 1 | E | O | A | I | U | | 1 | U | E | O | A | I |
| 2 | U | E | O | A | I | | 2 | I | U | E | O | A |
| 3 | I | U | E | O | A | | 3 | A | I | U | E | O |
| 4 | A | I | U | E | O | | 4 | O | A | I | U | E |
| 5 | O | A | I | U | E | | 5 | E | O | A | I | U |

<note>Note on the charts: The vowels rotate in a specific sequence across the five watches. By finding the current day and the current 'watch' (2-hour 24-minute block), a practitioner can identify which bird is in which state. For example, on Sunday during the 3rd watch, the Cock (E) is eating, while the Vulture (A) is ruling.</note>

It includes systematic reference tables for Sunday, Tuesday, Saturday, Friday, and Thursday, using vowels to track the cyclic rotation of the birds throughout the day.</summary>

## Auspiciousness of Shiva,

# Chapter Three,

<image-desc>An ornamental horizontal divider featuring a central oval within scrollwork and stylized leaves.</image-desc>

-> For the Seven Days of the Waxing Moon Phase <-

-> Night Cycles — *Kochakakkalippa* <note>A specific rhythmic meter in Tamil poetry characterized by four-line stanzas.</note> <-

-> The Eating Bird <note>Refers to the bird that begins the cycle by "eating" (the strongest state) at the start of the night.</note> <-

On Sunday and Tuesday, it is the Crow; on Monday and auspicious Wednesday, it is the Rooster...*
On Thursday, the golden one is the Peacock; on Friday, it is the prosperous Hawk...*
On Saturday, the Owl eats; thus one should know the order...*
These are the precious meanings uttered by the grace of the Vedic Sage <note>Refers to the Sage Agastya, the traditional progenitor of this science.</note>... (1)

-> *Nerisai Venba* <note>A classic Tamil poetic form with specific rhyme and alliteration rules.</note> — Terminal State Birds <-

In the twilight, for Tuesday and Sunday, the Crow is rare...*
For Wednesday and Monday, it is the Rooster; for Thursday, the Peacock—O lady wearing bangles...*
For Friday, it is the Hawk; for pure Saturday, it is the Owl...*
Thus do the birds stand in this order... (2)

-> Classifying the 7 Days and the 5 Night Watches <-
-> Classification. <-
-> For the 5 Letters, 5 Birds, and 5 Watches <-
-> (Sunday and Tuesday Night — *Nerisai Venba*) <-

The Crow eats; the Owl becomes the King;*
The graceful Hawk dies and falls—O lady, listen...*
The Peacock walks with yoga; the golden Rooster is in a state of sleep/clash...*
Observe and know this division with clarity... (3)

-> Monday and Wednesday Night <-

The Rooster eats; the sharp Crow becomes the King;*
The flourishing Owl dies and falls—O friend, listen...*
The Hawk walks; the great Peacock sleeps...*
The joy of conversation will increase there... (4)

-> Thursday Night <-

The Peacock eats; the spoken Rooster becomes the King;*
The Crow dies and falls, as we have seen—Therefore...*
The Owl walks; the Hawk sleeps, as they say...*
O lady with peaceful breasts, know and approach this... (5)

<sig>* Poetic elongation for meter</sig>

--- Page 27 ---
<meta>This page continues the specific calculations for the Waxing Moon phase (Shukla Paksha), moving from the general rules of the previous page into the specific avian cycles for Friday and Saturday nights. It concludes with a comprehensive reference table (Vakkia Chakram) that synthesizes the five activities of the birds across the entire week.</meta>

### Friday - Night

The Hawk eats, the great Peacock rules the kingdom... <note>original: "வல்லூறுமுண்ணமாமயிலுத்தாரைசா" - The "ruling" state indicates the bird is at its peak power.</note> ⁂
The wicked Rooster goes toward death—O good one, listen... ⁂
The Owl shall walk, and then it shall go to sleep... ⁂
The two states are joined in the flow... (6)

### Saturday - Night

The Owl eats its food, the Hawk rules as king... ⁂
The Peacock that joined them falls into death—O ladies wearing jewelry... ⁂
The Rooster walks; the Crow perched on the branch... ⁂
Falls with eyes closed into sleep... (7)

### Waxing Phase Night — Weekly Bird Classification

The bird standing in the **1st watch** eats; in the **2nd watch**, it becomes the **King**; in the **3rd watch**, it **Dies**; in the **4th watch**, it **Walks**; and in the **5th watch**, it **Sleeps**. Observe this. <note>A 'watch' (Sama or Yama) is a period of roughly 2 hours and 24 minutes, dividing the 12-hour night into five equal segments.</note>

### Cycle of Statements
<note>original: "வாக்கியசக்கரம்" (Vakkia Chakram) - A mnemonic grid used to quickly determine the bird's state without recalculating the verses.</note>

<image-desc>The following table is enclosed within a square decorative border composed of repeating circular floral woodcut patterns.</image-desc>

<margin>Waxing Moon Phase</margin>

| Mnemonic Verse | Days | Eat | King | Death | Walk | Sleep |
|----------|----------|----------|----------|----------|----------|----------|
| Ku-Ri-Pa<br>Po-The | Sunday,<br>Tuesday | 2<br>Owl | 4<br>Hawk | 3<br>Peacock | 1<br>Rooster | 5<br>Crow |

# The Science of the Five Birds
### (Panchapakshi Shastram)

-> **Night Birds for the Seven Days of the Week** <-

When examining the sub-periods of the watches <term>Sama Anthara Bhukti: the further division of a 2.4-hour "watch" into smaller segments of influence</term> during the Waxing Moon <term>Purva Paksha: the period of the brightening moon</term>, the night is treated similarly to the day. For one watch <note>Samam</note> consisting of 6 *nazhigai* <note>A nazhigai is 24 minutes; 6 nazhigai equals 2 hours and 24 minutes.</note>, the sequence of activities is determined as follows:

Eating: 1 ¼ | Walking: 1 ½ | Ruling: 2 | Sleeping: ¾ | Death: ½

It is certain that these activities will occur in this fixed order without variation.

-> **Alphabetical Calculation Charts** <-

Small pairs of dots serve as spacers in the grid.</image-desc>

# The Science of the Five Birds
### (Panchapakshi Shastram)

<image-desc>A complex astrological chart or "chakkaram" enclosed in a decorative rectangular border. The chart is divided into two main vertical sections. The left section is labeled "Saturn" <note>original: சனி (Sani)</note> and the right "Alternative method" <note>original: வேற்றுமுறை (Vettumurai)</note>. Both sections contain a grid with horizontal rows numbered 1 to 5 in Tamil numerals and vertical columns with headers identifying activities: "Watch," "Eating," "Ruling," "Death," "Walking," and "Sleeping." Each cell contains a Tamil numeral accompanied by a specific arrangement of dots, likely representing numerical values or astrological strengths.</image-desc>

->**The Chart of Night Activities during the Waxing Moon**<-
<note>Purva Paksha: the fifteen-day period of the brightening moon.</note>

---

->**Sunday — Tuesday**<-

| Watch <note>Samam: A period of 2 hours and 24 minutes.</note> | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 |
| :--- | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| **Crow** | Eating | Ruling | Death | Walking | Sleeping |
| **Owl** | Ruling | Death | Walking | Sleeping | Eating |
| **Hawk** | Death | Walking | Sleeping | Eating | Ruling |
| **Peacock** | Walking | Sleeping | Eating | Ruling | Death |
| **Rooster** | Sleeping | Eating | Ruling | Death | Walking |

---

->**Monday — Wednesday**<-

| Watch | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 |
| :--- | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| **Rooster** | Eating | Ruling | Death | Walking | Sleeping |
| **Crow** | Ruling | Death | Walking | Sleeping | Eating |
| **Owl** | Death | Walking | Sleeping | Eating | Ruling |
| **Hawk** | Walking | Sleeping | Eating | Ruling | Death |
| **Peacock** | Sleeping | Eating | Ruling | Death | Walking |

---

<note>In this system, "Eating" (Unndi) and "Ruling" (Arasu) are considered the most powerful and auspicious states for a bird, while "Death" (Saavu) represents a state of total inactivity or powerlessness. "Walking" (Nadai) and "Sleeping" (Thuyil) represent moderate and low-energy states respectively.</note>

# Chapter Four

<image-desc>Ornate horizontal graphic divider with floral and scrollwork motifs.</image-desc>

->## Verses for the Daytime of the Waning Moon<-
<note>original: அமரபக்ஷம் (amara-paksham); refers to the dark fortnight of the lunar cycle when the moon is waning.</note>
->### The Ruling Birds for the Seven Days<-
->(Nerisai Venba meter)<-
<note>A specific four-line poetic structure in Tamil literature known for its strict rhyme and rhythm.</note>

The Peacock, the Owl, the Rooster, and the Crow... ....................................
The Crow is for the rising Mercury <note>Wednesday</note>, O lady... .............................................
For the Priest <note>Jupiter/Thursday</note> the Owl, for Venus <note>Friday</note> the Hawk... .....................................
Always declare these for the daytime hours. .....................................................................

->(A Viruttham verse for the 60 units of time in the day and night)<-
<note>A unit of time called a nazhigai is 24 minutes; 60 nazhigai make a full 24-hour day.</note>

It is said: for Monday the Owl; for the noble Mars <note>Tuesday</note> the Hawk; .....................................
For the Sun <note>Sunday</note> the Crow; for Venus and Mercury the Peacock; .......................................
For Saturn and Jupiter the Rooster; thus it is spoken... ....................................
In those sixty units of time during the waning moon. ..............................................

->(The Five Actions of the Birds - Nerisai Venba meter)<-
<note>The Panchapakshi system assigns five activities to the birds: eating, walking, ruling, sleeping, and dying. Each activity predicts the success or failure of an endeavor.</note>

Eating, then dying, sleeping, ruling, and walking... ..........................................
Thus they move, O lady with modest eyes—but at night... ...........................................
Eating, sleeping, then walking, and then dying... ................................................
See that these are the feet of the observed path. ................................................

->Categorizing the 5 watches for the 7 days<-
->5 watches, 5 letters, and the outcomes for the birds<-
<note>A 'watch' (yaamam) is a three-hour period. The system uses five 'seed letters' (Aksharam) of the Tamil alphabet to calculate these cycles.</note>

->**Monday and Saturday—Daytime**<-

<image-desc>A large left curly brace groups the following four lines of text.</image-desc>
The striped Peacock eats; the Owl is dazed; .......................................................
Moving through all lands without staying—the great ......................................................
Rooster walks; the Hawk itself sleeps; .........................................
Because of this, the Crow dies. ........................................................... (4)

->**Thursday—Daytime**<-

<image-desc>A large left curly brace groups the following four lines of text.</image-desc>
See the Owl eating; the black Rooster walking; ..........................................
The Hawk, like a bee, acts as the King <note>rules</note>; the Crow .........................................
Closes its eyes <note>sleeps</note>; and the Peacock, ........................................
Seeking blame, dies; behold. .................................................................... (5)

->**Tuesday and Sunday—Daytime**<-

<image-desc>A large left curly brace groups the following four lines of text.</image-desc>
The eloquent Rooster eats; the following Hawk walks; ....................................
The good Crow rules well throughout the day; in the golden grove ......................................................
On the earth, the Peacock sleeps; the Owl is death; .......................................
Take this and consider it with your wisdom. ............................................................................ (6)

# The Science of the Five Birds

> Following that, the **Crow** shall walk—and then the **Peacock**............................*
> shall rule as the king of the land; after seeing his words, the **Rooster** shall sleep..........................*

---

->**Wednesday—Daytime**<-

> As the cool **Crow** eats, the solitary **Peacock** begins to walk.........................*
> Like a guardian of the soil, the **ruling power** arrives—O lady bedecked in vines!......................*
> While the **Rooster** sleeps, the strong **Hawk** must die...............................*
> Know that this is how Wednesday shall flourish...................................... (8)

---

->**Waning Moon Phase—Daytime**<-
<note>original: அமரபக்ஷம் (amara-paksham); the dark fortnight of the lunar month when the moon is shrinking.</note>

For the fifteen <term>Tithis</term> <note>lunar days</note> and the seven days of the week: for one day, out of thirty <term>Nazhigai</term> <note>a traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes; 30 nazhigai equals 12 hours of daylight</note>, there are five <term>Saamam</term> <note>a "watch" or period of time</note>. Therefore, one <term>Saamam</term> consists of six <term>Nazhigai</term> <note>approximately 2 hours and 24 minutes</note>.

<note>In this system, each bird (Hawk, Crow, Peacock, Rooster, and Owl) represents a specific vital force. Their activities—Eating, Walking, Ruling, Sleeping, and Dying—determine the auspiciousness of an hour for the person associated with that bird.</note>

# The Science of the Five Birds
<note>original: பஞ்சபட்சிசாத்திரம் (Pancha-pakshi-shastram); a system of biorhythms or Vedic astrology based on five birds representing five elements and their changing states of power.</note>

->**Daytime Birds for the Seven Days of the Week**<-

The bird that **eats** during the first <term>Saamam</term> <note>a "watch" or period of time lasting 144 minutes</note> shall **die** <note>signifying a state of total powerlessness or inactivity</note> in the 2nd <term>Saamam</term>, **sleep** in the 3rd <term>Saamam</term>, **rule** <note>state of peak power</note> in the 4th <term>Saamam</term>, and **walk** in the 5th <term>Saamam</term>. Furthermore, in the internal divisions of these periods, during the <term>Purva Paksha</term> <note>the waxing phase of the moon</note>, just like the daytime: Eating, Walking, Ruling, Sleeping, and Death occur. Understand that for every 6 <term>Nazhigai</term> <note>a traditional unit of 24 minutes; 6 nazhigai equals one saamam</note>, these activities will occur in an unchanging cycle.

## The Alphabetical System Chart
<note>original: எழுத்துமுறைச்சக்கரம் (Ezhuthu-murai-chakkaram); a lookup grid where the five birds are assigned specific vowels to track their movements through time.</note>

# The Science of the Five Birds
<note>original: பஞ்சபட்சசாஸ்திரம் (Pancha-pakshi-shastram); a traditional system of biorhythms used in South India to determine auspicious times based on five birds that represent different elements and planetary influences.</note>

<note>The five activities represent states of vitality: **Eating** (Undi) is gaining strength, **Walking** (Nadai) is active effort, **Ruling** (Arasu) is peak success/power, **Sleeping** (Thuyil) is vulnerability, and **Death** (Saavu) is a total lack of power or "inauspiciousness" for that bird at that time.</note>

<note>original: பஞ்சபட்சிசாஸ்திரம் (Pancha-pakshi-shastram). This system divides the day into five equal parts called 'Saamams.' ## Chapter Five

---

->Verses for the Night activities of the Seven Days of the Waning Moon.<-
<note>original: அமரபட்சம் (Amara-paksham), the waning phase of the moon, also known as Krishna Paksha.</note>

->The Eating Birds<-

->(Verse in the six-foot long meter)<-
<note>original: அறுசீர்க்கழிநெடிலடியாசிரியவிருத்தம் (Aru-seer-kazhinedilaadi-aasiriya-viruttham). A traditional Tamil poetic form used for instructional texts.</note>

On the days of Saturn and the Moon, the Rooster eats; on Friday, the Peacock eats;*
On Mercury's day, the Owl takes its meal; on Jupiter's day, the Crow consumes its prey;*
On the day of the Sun, the Hawk joyfully takes its food...*
We have stated these in order for the waning phase that appears; learn them well. (1)

->The Dying Birds<-

On Jupiter's day, the Rooster; on Mercury's day, the Crow loses its life...*
For the Sun, the Hawk; for the Great Sage (the Moon), the Owl...*
For Mars, the Peacock; they remain in the state of death at the start of the night...*
In the waning phase, oh lady with hair like the instrument's curve, speak having known their place. (2)

Division for the 7 days and the 5 night-watches.
The results for the 5 letters, 5 birds, and 5 watches.
<note>The '5 letters' refer to the vowels A, I, U, E, O, which are linked to the five birds: Hawk, Owl, Crow, Rooster, and Peacock.</note>

->Monday and Saturday Nights<-

->(Verse in the Nerisai Venpa meter)<-

{ The Rooster roams to eat; the Owl...*
{ ...walks; the Great Peacock rules the land;*
{ The Crow sleeps; the Hawk dies...*
{ Oh one learned in the arts, see how they revolve. (3)

->Friday Night<-

<margin>2</margin>
{ In the night, the Peacock eats; the Crow walks with intent...*
{ The Hawk rules the earth; the Rooster...*
{ ...sleeps; the Owl dies; oh lady whose speech is like the swan's gait...*
{ ...and who wears fine jewels, what distress could then come? (4)

<meta>This page continues the poetic instructions for the activities of the five birds during the night watches of the waning moon phase. While the previous page began the night-time verses for Monday, Saturday, and Friday, this page covers Wednesday, Thursday, Tuesday, and Sunday, and provides a comprehensive reference table (Vakkiya Chakkaram) for the entire waning period at night.</meta>

### Wednesday—Night

<margin>3</margin>
> When the Great Sage eats, the black Crow walks...*
> To rule the world as a cruel king—with a smile...*
> The Hawk sleeps, the red Rooster dies...*
> Oh, the Cuckoo in the mango grove, thus says my mind. (5)
<note>original: முனியுண்ண (Muni-un-na). "Muni" (Sage) is a cryptic poetic term for the Owl.</note>

### Thursday—Night

<margin>4</margin>
> Say, the Crow eats, while the strong Hawk walks...*
> The Rooster rules without flaw—those without equal...*
> The Great Owl sees sleep, and the living Peacock dies...*
> Oh, women with eyes shaped like the Sel-fish, speak thus. (6)
<note>original: சேலார்விழிமடவீர் (Sel-aar-vizhi-madaveer). A common poetic address to women with "fish-like eyes," symbolizing beauty.</note>

### Tuesday-Sunday—Night

<margin>5</margin>
> While the Hawk eats pleasantly, the long Rooster walks...*
> The good Owl arrives to rule the land—the wicked...*
> The Peacock falls into sleep, and the Crow falls and dies...*
> Like a mountain attacked and turned to dust. (7)

### Schedule of the Waning Moon Phase—Weekly Division

The bird that rises in the first watch (Jamam) will **Eat**. In the 2nd watch, it will **Sleep**. In the 3rd watch, it will **Walk**. In the 4th watch, it will **Die**. In the 5th watch, it will **Rule**.
<note>A 'Jamam' or watch is a period of approximately 2 hours and 24 minutes. This sequence defines the strength of the bird throughout the night.</note>

| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Pe - Ni | Monday | 7 Rooster | 2 Owl | 9 Peacock | 3 Crow | 1 Hawk |
| Ko - Du - Va | Saturday | 9 Peacock | 3 Crow | 1 Hawk | 7 Rooster | 2 Owl |
| Ko - Mu - Va<br/>Pe - Ni | Friday | 1 Hawk | 7 Rooster | 2 Owl | 9 Peacock | 3 Crow |
| Aa - So - Ni<br/>Ro - Du | Tuesday<br/>Sunday | 2 Owl | 9 Peacock | 3 Crow | 1 Hawk | 7 Rooster |
| Ni - Po - Ku<br/>Ta - - Ve | Wednesday | 3 Crow | 1 Hawk | 7 Rooster | 2 Owl | 9 Peacock |
| Ku - t Te Ve<br/>Ni - Po | Thursday | 3 Crow | 1 Hawk | 7 Rooster | 2 Owl | 9 Peacock |

### Weekly Cycles—Day and Night

In this system of logic <note>original: "Sarmantha Yukti"</note>, as in the waxing moon phase, the core activities do not change. For the first <term>Jamam</term> <note>A watch of approximately 2.4 hours</note>, the duration is divided as follows: Eating takes 15, Walking 12, Ruling 9, Sleeping 12, and Death 12—totaling 60 <term>Nazhigai</term> <note>A traditional unit of time; 60 nazhigai equals 24 hours</note>. It should be observed that across the five watches, the sequence of these activities shifts according to a specific rotation.

# Science of the Five Birds
### Page 26

# With God’s Grace.

## Chapter Six.

->The Waxing Moon Phase<-
<note>original: "Purva Paksha" — the fifteen days from the new moon to the full moon.</note>
->The Results of the Birds’ Five Activities<-
->A Poem in the Six-Foot Meter<-
<note>original: "Aru-seer-kazhinedil-asiriya-viruttam" — a traditional Tamil poetic structure characterized by six feet per line.</note>

->The Result of Eating<-
<note>In the "Five Birds" system, "Eating" is a state of high vitality and positive outcomes.</note>

Abundant food will be gained, and those who left the town will return; anxieties will fade . . . *
Rain will fall upon the living earth; there will be no theft, and the fruits of one's labor will shine . . . *
If spoken of, a daughter will be born; illnesses will be cured, and a woman will find fulfillment . . . *
Fine gold and commerce will flourish, O King! O forest cuckoo with a sweet voice! (1)
<note>The poet addresses a "cuckoo" or a woman with beautiful hair as a poetic convention common in Tamil didactic literature.</note>

->The Result of Walking<-
<note>"Walking" represents a state of moderate activity but instability.</note>

If it is "walking," stability will be lost; enemies will triumph and great wealth will depart . . . *
The fruits of one's physical labor will not shine, and the lives of young women will not be good . . . *
Even for a moment, wealth will not accumulate; desires will crawl slowly and marriage will not occur *
As the secret methods were told, so the elders have spoken, O one with curling locks! (2)

->The Result of Ruling<-
<note>"Ruling" is the most powerful and auspicious state in this system.</note>

If it is "ruling," one gains land; through the government, benefits will be attained . . . *
The sound of the victory drum and the honors of the bow will be yours; the tasks undertaken will shine . . . *
Gifts and the friendship of great elders will be gained; old illnesses will vanish and there is no fear . . . *
Faults will disappear, stolen items will be found, and there is no loss, O one with beautiful hair! (3)

->The Result of Sleeping<-
<note>"Sleeping" is a state of dormancy where no new ventures should be started.</note>

If it is "sleeping," disease will not change; those who have gone far away will not return . . . *
In that state, deeds will not turn out well; wisdom and one's way of life will decline . . . *
Evil will persist, the clouds will not pour rain, and the red paddy crops will fail; this is bad *
Debts will rise and will not be repaid; we have spoken this as the truth. (4)

->The Result of Deep Sleep<-
<note>original: "Thunjum palan" — literally "the result of dying" or "deepest sleep." This is the most inauspicious state.</note>

If it is "deep sleep," it speaks of death; great illness will also befall the head . . . *
The objects desired in the heart will not be joined; wealth will not be seen, and stability will crumble . . . *
Afflictions and diseases will greatly increase; the master of the house will feel disgust and will not find renunciation . . . *
O one with hair adorned with lotus flowers! Know that this is as it was thoughtfully spoken. (5)

->The Same for the Waning Moon Phase<-
<note>original: "Apara Paksha" — the fifteen days from the full moon to the new moon.</note>
->The Result of Eating<-

Loss will not be seen immediately; life and gains will be attained along with one's kin . . . *
Quickly, illnesses will be cured, and there is not even a little confusion . . . *
Heavy, continuous rain will be abundant; the nature of the house will be filled with light . . . *
Though there is no immediate result, it will be attained after the time spoken of. (6)

<summary>This page outlines the astrological predictions for the "Five Birds" system, detailing how five states—Eating, Walking, Ruling, Sleeping, and Deep Sleep—affect various aspects of life like health, wealth, and success during different lunar phases.</summary>
<keywords>Pancha Pakshi, Waxing Moon (Purva Paksha), Waning Moon (Apara Paksha), Tamil Astrology, Divination, Omens, Health predictions, Wealth</keywords>

--- Page 46 ---
<meta>Continuing from the previous page, which introduced the first two states—Eating and Walking—of the "Five Birds" (Pancha Pakshi) system during the waxing moon phase. This page concludes the descriptions of the five activities and begins explaining how these states correspond to human characteristics like age and appearance.</meta>

# The Science of the Five Birds

### The Result of the Journey
<note>In the Pancha Pakshi system, "Walking" or "Journeying" (Nadai) indicates a time of movement and moderate progress, though the results here are described as surprisingly positive compared to some interpretations.</note>

Victory and happiness will arise, and illnesses will be cured . . . *
There is no fear regarding the tasks undertaken; their status will not diminish . . . *
No loss shall be seen; there will be heavy rain; even those from afar . . . *
Will arrive together if the "walking" of the bird is predicted . . . (7)

### The Result of Ruling
<note>The "Ruling" (Arasu) state is the most powerful and auspicious, representing peak influence and command.</note>

There is success and comfort; in a distinguished journey . . . *
There will be light rain from the clouds; there is no confusion on the earth . . . *
By its nature, victory and long-lasting profit can be obtained . . . *
Fear will not arise daily if the bird is "ruling" . . . (8)

### The Result of Drowsiness
<note>Also called "Sleeping" (Thuyil), this state represents a period of inactivity, vulnerability, and stagnation.</note>

Besides the illness you ponder, another ailment will increase . . . *
Even if the season is right, there will be no rain; you will see losses in what you have kept . . . *
Actions will be performed with a weary and troubled heart . . . *
Only sins will arise if the bird is "sleeping" . . . (9)

### The Result of Death
<note>"Death" (Chavu) in this context refers to a state of total powerlessness or the "dead" phase of a cycle, where no new initiatives should be taken.</note>

There will be loss in the gardens; all actions will turn to conflict . . . *
Apart from the removal of past sins, no new good deeds will bear fruit . . . *
Because the bird has fallen into "death" and sits in agitation . . . *
O woman, most excellent in this world, thus do the bird-treatises say . . . (10)
<note>The poet continues the convention of addressing a noble woman as the listener of the wisdom.</note>

### The Subtle Bird
->A Poem in the Nerisai Venpa Meter<-
<note>The "Subtle Bird" (Sukshma Pakshi) refers to the hidden transitions or micro-states within the main periods. The *Nerisai Venpa* is a classic four-line Tamil poetic form known for its strict rhyme and rhythm.</note>

If we speak of it, one bird eats while another's life departs . . . *
One travels as if nothing else exists—In justice, hear me . . . *
Graciously, the world knows that one shall sleep . . . *
Starting from the first, the world . . . (11)

### The Result of Age
<note>This section maps the bird's five activities to the stages of human life, suggesting that the "vibe" of the activity matches the energy of that age group.</note>

The one in the "Eating" state is an elder; the one who "Walks" is a youth . . . *
The one on the "Ruling" throne is a tall, mature man—becoming tainted . . . *
The one who "Sleeps" is a short person, and if it becomes agitated . . . *
Know that "Death" is like a child of one year . . . (12)
<term>Age Correspondence: Eating = Elder, Walking = Youth, Ruling = Adult, Sleeping = Short/Stunted, Death = Infant.</term>

### The Result of Portions
<note>"Angisha" refers to the specific qualities or social roles assigned to each state.</note>

The one who eats is a child; the "Walking" one is a prince . . . *
The one with the quality of "Ruling" is the King who protects the earth . . . *
The one who "Sleeps" is an old man; the one who "Dies" is one who has attained Silence . . . *
This is the fruit of the penance one holds . . . (13)
<note>original: "Monamudithon" — literally, one who has completed a vow of silence (Mouna), often referring to a renunciant or sage.</note>

### Results of Physical Observation
->A Poem in the Long-Footed Verse<-
<note>This section describes the physical appearance or "temperament" (Vevvan - fierce/hot) associated with the bird's current state.</note>

The one high in "Eating" is fierce; the one in the pure "Walking" state is short and fierce . . . *
The one with fearful hair is fierce in the "Ruling" state . . . *

<summary>This page details the specific outcomes of the bird states—Walking, Ruling, Sleeping, and Death—while also introducing how these astrological states correspond to human ages, social ranks (from child to king), and physical traits.</summary>
<keywords>Pancha Pakshi, Astrology, Ruling state, Sleeping state, Death state, Tamil meters, Venpa, Viruttam, Age correspondences, Royal roles</keywords>

--- Page 47 ---
<meta>Continuing from page 27, which detailed the negative outcomes of the "Death" state in the Five Birds system. This page completes those observations and moves into the comparative results—how different states (Eating, Walking, Ruling, Sleeping, Death) interact when two people or forces encounter each other.</meta>

# The Science of the Five Birds
<note>original: "பஞ்சபட்சிசாஸ்திரம்" (Pancha Pakshi Shastram). An ancient system of Tamil astrology where five birds represent five rhythmic states of human energy.</note>

**Page 28**

...Between two people, even in that state, the one who is stronger will prevail...
Is it necessary to say? Even in "Death," the stronger of the two will prevail... (12)

->**The Result of Strength** — (Nerishai Venpa Meter)<-
<note>Nerishai Venpa is a specific classical four-line poetic meter in Tamil literature.</note>

Strength is found in "Eating," and certainty in the "Walking" and "Ruling" states... ... ...*
If one is "Sleeping," but is cherished and looked after well... ... .. ...*
For the one with flowing hair <note>A poetic reference to a woman</note>, birth will not be ill-fated... ... ..*
The words of the auspicious and heavy... (15)

->**The Result of Victory**<-

The one "Eating" will conquer the one "Walking"; the one "Walking"... ... ...*
Will even destroy the "Ruler" if they collide—the great... ... ..*
The "Ruler" will conquer the one "Sleeping"; the one "Sleeping"... ... ... *
Will conquer the one "Eating," so says the wisdom... (16)
<note>This describes a "rock-paper-scissors" style cycle of dominance between the five states: Eating > Walking > Ruling > Sleeping > Eating.</note>

->**The Result of Activity Between Two Parties**<-
->(Arushir Kazhinediladi Ashiriyaviruttham Meter)<-
<note>A complex meter consisting of six-foot long lines.</note>

If the elder is "Eating," he shall win; if "Walking," the younger shall win... ... . .
If it is "Ruling," the superior one will greatly conquer... ... .*
If both are "Sleeping," the younger will always win... ... ... *
If it is the wretched "Death," the two warriors shall not return... (17)

->**The Result of the Phase**<-

If both names correspond to a bird that is "Eating" ... .... ..*
Then that very bird wins; in "Walking," the taller one wins.. ... .*
In the intent of "Ruling," the elder wins; in "Sleeping," the one who sleeps wins*
The great sages have said that in "Death," it is equal, Oh Lady!... (18)
<note>The address "Oh Lady" (Mathe) is a traditional poetic device where the author addresses a woman to explain the science.</note>

->**The Result of Combined Actions**<-
->(Nerishai Venpa Meter)<-

The one "Eating" will drive away the one "Walking"; both of them... .. ... *
Will drive away the ruler of the town; Oh woman... .... *
Those who are dying will fear the one who is "Sleeping"... ... ... *
He will call out to them, "Do not fear!"... (19)

## Chapter Seven

### The Waxing Moon Phase — The Colors of the Birds
<note>original: "Purva Paksham" (பூர்வபட்சம்). The fifteen days from the New Moon to the Full Moon.</note>

->The Nerishai Venpa Meter<-
<note>A classical four-line Tamil poetic form characterized by a specific rhythmic break in the second line.</note>

The Hawk is the color of gold; the growing Owl is white . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The Crow, filled with movement, is red — listen, O good lady! . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The Cock is a vibrant green; the cool Peacock is black . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
Speak these so their fragrance fills the measurement of time. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . (1)

### The Waning Moon Phase — The Colors of the Birds
<note>original: "Apara Paksham" (அபரபட்சம்). The fifteen days from the Full Moon to the New Moon.</note>

The Owl is red; the jewel-like Crow is golden in hue . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The Peacock is of a well-formed green color — O women wearing jewels! . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The Cock is white; and the Hawk is known . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
By a clear and prominent black. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃

### The Waxing Moon Phase — The Categories of the Birds
<note>original: "Sathi Pakshi" (சாதிப்பட்சி). This refers to the classification of the birds into the four-fold social structure (varna) common in ancient Indian systems.</note>

The Hawk is the Priest; the growing Owl is the Merchant . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The Crow, full of movement, is the King of the world — O good one, listen! . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The Cock is the Farmer; and the Peacock is called the Outcaste . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
Know this by studying the texts of the Lord of the Discus. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . (2)
<note>Lord of the Discus: A reference to the deity Vishnu.</note>

### The Waning Moon Phase — The Categories of the Birds

The Hawk is the golden Priest; the white Owl is the King . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The Crow of many words is the Merchant — established . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The Cock is the one who holds the plow (the Farmer); and the one who rules the day . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
Is called the Outcaste (the Peacock), thus it is sung. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . (3)

### The Phases — Mineral, Plant, and Animal Classifications
<note>original: "Dhatu, Moola, Jeeva" (தாது மூல சீவன்). A triadic classification in Siddha science: Dhatu (Mineral/Inorganic), Moola (Plant/Root), and Jeeva (Animal/Organic life).</note>

The Hawk is the Root (Plant); the growing Owl is the Life (Animal) . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The agile Crow is the Mineral . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The Cock is of the Root-Life nature; with the beautiful Peacock being Mineral . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
These are spoken of as the living Roots. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃

### The Lexicon of Bogar — The Alchemical Birds
<note>Bogar was a legendary Tamil Siddha (alchemist and sage) credited with many esoteric works.</note>

->The Enshir Kazhinediladi Ashiriyaviruttham Meter<-
<note>A meter with eight-foot long lines, often used for more complex or technical descriptions.</note>

Listen as I speak of the Five Birds and their Alchemical Minerals... . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The spread-out senses and the five elements... . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The Bird of the painting is a brilliant red... . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
It is the superior mineral of five colors... . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The golden puppet that descends through the yellow clouds... . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The maiden prepared within the great glass vial... . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
<note>This final section appears to link the Five Birds system to Siddha alchemy, using metaphorical language like "maiden within the vial" to describe chemical reactions or mercury preparations.</note>

# The Science of the Five Birds
### Page 40

A small amount of gold within this... . . . . . . . . ❃
Even the horse and all else shall become the bird. . . . . (6)

->**The Waxing Moon — The Elemental Birds**<-
->The Nerishai Venpa Meter<-

<note>original: "Purva Paksha" (பூர்வபட்சம்). The bright half of the lunar month. "Bhuta Pakshi" (பூதப்பட்சி) refers to the birds' association with the five great elements (Mahabhutas).</note>

The Hawk is the element of Air; the waning Owl is Earth . . . . . . . . . ❃
The wicked Crow is of the Fire element—listen, O good lady! . . . . . ❃
The Cock is Water; and the Peacock is the Ether of Space . . . . . . . . . ❃
O you with hair as dark as rain-clouds, speak of these. . . . . . . . . . . (7)

->**The Waning Moon — The Elemental Birds**<-
<note>original: "Apara Paksha" (அபரபட்சம்). The dark half of the lunar month.</note>

The Owl is Earth; the Crow is the Fire . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The Hawk is of the great element of Air . . . . . . . . ❃
The red-crested Cock is the element of Water . . . . ❃
The Peacock is the vastness of the Ether. . . . . . . . . . . . (8)

->**The Vital Measurement of the Birds**<-
<note>original: "Murugu Pakshi" (முருகுயட்சி). This refers to the numerical power or "matra" (units) assigned to each bird, which determines their relative strength.</note>

The Crow’s vital measure is one full unit; the Hawk is three-quarters . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The Owl is half a unit; the Cock is one-quarter . . . . . . ❃
The beautiful Peacock is one-eighth of a unit . . . . . . ❃
Know that these are the vital measures of those who live. . . . . . . . . . . . (9)

->**The Hierarchy of Dominance**<-
<note>original: "Maintha Paksham" (மாய்ந்தபட்சம்). Literally "the dead/extinguished phase," referring to which bird overpowers or "kills" another in the astrological hierarchy.</note>

The Cock is stronger than the Peacock . . . . ❃
The Owl is stronger than the Cock . . . . . ❃
The Hawk is stronger than the Owl . . . . . ❃
And the Crow is stronger than the Hawk, see how it turns! . . . . . . . . (10)

### The Enemy Birds

->The Verse of Six-Foot Lines<-
<note>original: "Aruseer Kazhinediladi Asiriya Virutham." A rhythmic poetic form used for long descriptive passages.</note>

The Hawk is the enemy of the Peacock; and for that Cock, it is the same . . . . ❃
The Crow is the enemy of the Owl; and for the Crow, the Owl is the enemy . . ❃
The Cock and the Hawk are enemies to the Crow . . . . . ❃
These are the life-enemies; all others are considered friends, O King. . . . . (11)

->**The Allied Birds — The Nerishai Venpa Meter**<-
<note>original: "Uravu Pakshi" (உறவுபட்சி). Birds that are compatible or "friendly" in the calculation.</note>

The Peacock and the Hawk are allies; the Crow and the Cock . . . . . . . . ❃
The Hawk and the Crow are also joined—O you with eyes like blades! . . . . ❃
They say the Cock and the Peacock share a friendship;
So it is agreed by those experts who do not deviate from the texts. . . . . . . . . (12)

### The Time Birds
<note>original: "Kala Pakshi" (காலப்பட்சி). This refers to the birds' governance over specific periods or their role in indicating travel and time.</note>

The Owl represents the time that has already passed . . . . ❃
The Cock speaks of the time of those traveling abroad . . . . ❃
The Hawk and the Crow show the time that is yet to come . . . . . . . ❃
This is the manner described by the powerful ones. . . . . . . . . (13)

The dark Crow is the hexagon—O beautiful lady! . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃

These are the words spoken by the Lord of the Ages. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . (14)

->**(The Birds of Tastes)**<-

The Owl is sweet; the Cock is slightly sour . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The esteemed black Crow is saltiness—O auspicious one! . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The Hawk and the Peacock are said to be bitter . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
O you with hair as dark as the night, know this. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . (15)

->**(The Birds of the Landscapes)**<-
<note>These refer to the "Ainthinai," the five ecological zones of classical Tamil poetry, each associated with specific moods and deities.</note>

The Hawk is the parched desert; the Owl is the fertile mountains . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The good Crow is the riverside farmlands—O delicate lady! . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The Cock is the forest; the thriving Peacock is the seashore . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
Know this through the words of the Vedic seers. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . (16)

The Hawk is the movement of water and flowers . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The Owl of great strength is spoken of as four-legged . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
The Cock is two-legged; the Peacock bird is of the wind . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
Observe these attributes and then speak your divination. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . (17)
