Cock is bronze; and the beautiful Peacock is iron . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
May you live long, O women, as you learn these types. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . (18)

->**(The Birds of Omens)**<-

The Hawk is coming—the feathers of the great mountain dweller . . . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃

# Signs of Misfortune
## In the meter of Six-foot Long Verse

If the ruler is the Hawk, say he shall separate the people . . . . . . *
If a word is heard, say it is like something being taken and broken . . . . . . *
If a theft occurs, say a single person has taken the object . . . . . . *
If the colorful Cock is active, it will be as dust and hair, O lady! . . . . . . (20)
<note>These are omens (nimittas). The text describes how to interpret events based on which "bird" is dominant at the moment of an inquiry.</note>

### Young Birds — In the meter of Virtuous Venpa

The Hawk, the Crow, and the Peacock—these three are male . . . . . . . . . . *
We have declared their good colors and their governing planets—but the others are bad . . . . . . *
The Owl and the Golden Cock are female, O friend! . . . . . . *
Seek them out and learn the skill of speaking their truths . . . . . . . . . . . (21)
<note>The "Golden Cock" refers to the wild or ritualized form of the rooster in this system.</note>

### The Waxing Moon — Directional Birds
<term>Waxing Moon (Purva Paksha): The fifteen-day period from the New Moon to the Full Moon.</term>

The Hawk is in the East; the Owl is in the South . . . . . . . . . *
The dark Crow resides in the West—without any fault . . . . . . . . . *
The Cock, who swallows its prey, is in the North . . . . . . . *
And the Peacock stands in the center. . . . . . . . . . . . . . (22)

### The Waning Moon — Directional Birds
<term>Waning Moon (Apara Paksha): The fifteen-day period from the Full Moon to the New Moon.</term>

The green Peacock is in the West; on this earth, the Owl is in the North . . . . . . . . *
The Hawk is in the East—worthy of praise . . . . . . . . . *
The Crow is in the South—O lady with eyes like honey! . . . . . . . *
The Golden Cock is revealed to be in the center. . . . . . . . . . . . (23)

### The Same for the New Moon Period

The Hawk is in the center; the flourishing Owl is in the South . . . . . . . . *
The excellent Crow is in the East where wealth resides—and slightly to the left . . . . . . . . *
The Cock is in the South; the grand Peacock is in the West . . . . . . . . *
This is for the purpose of declaring the results at all times. . . . . . . . . . . . . . (24)

### From Another Siddha’s Text — The Five-Letter Chakra

Northeast, <unclear>Maiden</unclear>, facing North, then Southeast and West . . . . . . . . . *
The Wind direction, the Void, and the East—difficult to speak of . . . . . . . . . *
In the Southeast and in the North is the interior—A, I, U, E, O . . . . . . . . *
This is the chakra of the five letters. . . . . . . . . . . . . . (25)
<note>The "five letters" refer to the primary vowels of the Tamil alphabet (அ, இ, உ, எ, ஒ) which are mapped to the five birds (Hawk, Owl, Crow, Cock, Peacock).</note>

<meta>Continuing from page 1, which established the basic characteristics and gender of the five birds (Hawk, Owl, Crow, Cock, and Peacock) and their directional placements during the waxing moon. This page introduces the "Sound-Bird" system, where the initial vowel of a person's name or their first spoken word is used to assign a specific bird to different parts of the body for divining their current state.</meta>

<page-num>2</page-num>

<image-desc>Decorative floral horizontal border at the top of the page.</image-desc>

->With God's Help<-

# Chapter Eight

<image-desc>Ornate horizontal decorative divider with a central floral motif.</image-desc>

->Converting the phonetic syllables of the inquirer’s spoken words into birds,<-
->examining their standing based on the lines touched,<-
->and classifying them to declare the results.<-
<note>This refers to the practice of "Pancha Pakshi Shastra" (The Science of the Five Birds), where the five primary vowels of the Tamil language are linked to five birds to predict a person's fortune.</note>

### The Sound of 'A'

->In the meter of Virtuous Venpa<-

The one named with the sound of 'A' has the Hawk at the head...................*
The Owl is seen at the neck, and the Crow is cherished at the hands...................*
The body is the Cock, and the Peacock is at the front and back...................*
The two legs are these five vowels: A-I-U-E-O.....................(1)
<note>original: "அ-இ-உ-எ-ஓ" (A-I-U-E-O). In this system, the five vowels represent the vital breath or cycle of the birds.</note>

### The Sound of 'I'

The one named with the sound of 'I' has the Owl at the head...................*
The Crow is at the neck, and the hands are the Cock...................*
The body is the Peacock, and the Hawk is at the front and back...................*
The legs are these five vowels: I-U-E-O-A........................(2)
<note>Note the shift in the cycle: the bird that was at the neck in the previous verse now moves to the head.</note>

### The Sound of 'U'

The day named with the sound of 'U' begins at the head................*
The Cock is at the neck, and the Peacock is at the hands...................*
The Hawk is at the front and back, and the Owl lives at the body...............*
The good legs are the Crow: U-E-O-A-I...................(3)

### The Sound of 'E'

The one in this land named with the sound of 'E' has the Crow at the head................*
The hands are the Peacock, and the body is the Hawk...................*
The front and back are the Owl, and the Cock's legs are great.................*
As the good scholars recite: E-O-A-I-U.......................(4)

### The Sound of 'O'

The day named with the sound of 'O' has the Cock at the head................*
The hands are the Hawk, and the body is the Owl...................*
The Crow is on the earth, at the front and back...................*
The Peacock is the legs—behold, O moon-like lady! O-A-I-U-E......................(5)
<term>Moon-like lady: A poetic address often used in Tamil didactic literature to engage the reader, implying a person of wisdom or beauty.</term>

<summary>This page details the phonetic system of the "Five Birds" divination, mapping the five primary vowels (A, I, U, E, O) to specific birds and then assigning those birds to parts of the human body (Head, Neck, Hands, Body, Legs) to calculate omens.</summary>
<keywords>phonetic syllables, vowels, Hawk, Owl, Crow, Cock, Peacock, body mapping, divination, Pancha Pakshi, Venpa meter</keywords>

--- Page 53 ---
<meta>Continuing from page 2, which detailed how the five birds are assigned to different parts of the body based on the initial vowel sounds of a person's name ('A' and 'I'). This page shifts focus to the "Amrita Sthana" (Nectar or Life-Force Locations), tracking how the vital energy of each bird moves through the body according to the lunar days (Tithis) of the month.</meta>

<page-num>46</page-num>
<header>Panchapakshi Shastra</header>

->The Ornamentation of the Life-Force Locations<-

<margin>The rise and fall of the Life-Force state during the waxing and waning lunar fortnights.</margin>
By these treasures of knowledge, the positions from the feet to the head are to be known.
<note>original: "பதாந்திகேசம்" (Padanthikesam). A Sanskrit-derived term meaning 'from the feet to the hair/head'.</note>

->First Day of the Lunar Fortnight — The Sole — In the meter of Straight Venpa<-
<note>The 'First Day' refers to *Prathamai*, the first day after the New or Full Moon.</note>
The Hawk is the sole of the foot, the Owl is the ankle...................*
The Crow is the leg that rises above, O young ones...................*
The Cock is the lower thigh, O lady with the humble waist...................*
Declare that the Peacock resides at the crown...................(6)
<note>original: "வல்லூறு" (Valluru/Hawk), "ஆந்தை" (Aandhai/Owl), "வெண்காகம்" (Venkagam/White Crow), "கோழி" (Kozhi/Cock), "மயில்" (Mayil/Peacock).</note>

->Second Day — The Ankle<-
Know, my friend, that the Hawk is the ankle...................*
The Owl is the forelock upon the head—this is correct...................*
The Crow is the leg that rises in beauty, the Cock is the thigh...................*
Place the Peacock at the beautiful genitals...................(7)
<note>The second lunar day is called *Thudigai*.</note>

->Third Day — The Lower Leg<-
The Hawk is the lower leg, the Owl is the crest on the head...................*
The Crow is the knee that swells, and when calculated...................*
The Cock is the thigh, the Peacock is the gentle one...................*
Behold them at the end of the garment at the waist...................(8)
<note>The third lunar day is called *Thrithigai*.</note>

->Fourth Day — The Knee and Waist<-
The Hawk is the hip, the Owl is the growing lower back...................*
The Crow is the upper back—hard to describe...................*
The Cock is the nape of the neck, the Peacock is the shoulder blade...................*
Tell your friend that these are the sharp placements...................(9)
<note>The fourth lunar day is called *Chathurthi*.</note>

->Fifth Day — The Thigh and Navel<-
The Hawk is the navel, the Owl is the stomach, the Crow is the chest...................*
The Cock is the soft breasts, like a flowering vine...................*
The beautiful Peacock is under the arms, O lady with dark tresses...................*
The celestial ones knew this and spoke it to the world...................(10)
<note>The fifth lunar day is called *Panchami*.</note>

->Sixth Day — The Buttocks and Lower Abdomen<-
The Hawk is the lower abdomen, the Owl is the stomach...................*
The Crow is the thriving chest, O lady with the bright forehead...................*
The Cock is the base where one sits, the Peacock is the ribs...................*
Declare that they reside there without fail...................(11)
<note>The sixth lunar day is called *Sashti*.</note>

->Seventh Day — The Waist and Lower Back<-
The Hawk is the lower back, the Owl is the rising upper back...................*
The Crow is the thriving nape of the neck, O bright-browed one...................*
The Cock is the skin of the right ribs, the Peacock is on the left...................*
Declare that they reside there among the ribs...................(12)
<note>The seventh lunar day is called *Sapthami*.</note>

<meta>Continuing from page 44, which described the locations of the "vital energy" or "breath" (Amrita Sthana) for the first through seventh days of the lunar fortnight. This page continues the progression of the five birds—Hawk, Owl, Crow, Cock, and Peacock—moving through the human body as the lunar cycle advances toward the full moon.</meta>

<page-num>45</page-num>
<header>Panchapakshi Shastra</header>

### Eighth Day — Navel and Stomach

* The Hawk is the stomach, and the Owl is the chest ...... *
The Crow, difficult to describe, is the back — how base ........... *
The Cock is the side-ribs, the beautiful Peacock ........... *
The Cock is the right ribs, so it is said ..................... (13)
<note>The eighth day of the lunar cycle is called *Ashtami*. In this system, each bird represents a different concentration of life force.</note>

### Ninth Day — Stomach and Chest

The unblinking chest is the chest, the Cock and the Owl ... *
The whole of the spine is the Cock — through long practice ..... *
The Crow is the legs, and with the Kingly Crow in this world ... *
The superior text contains this ..................... (14)
<note>The ninth day is *Navami*. The text emphasizes that these associations are part of a "superior" or "ancient" lineage of knowledge.</note>

### Tenth Day — The Hand

The two hands are the Hawk, the neck is the Owl ........ *
The true spine is the Cock — slowly ... ....... *
The two thighs are the Peacock, study the words of the mouth *
Declare this as the form for human beings ................. (15)
<note>The tenth day is *Dashami*. Note how the birds shift from internal organs to the limbs.</note>

### Eleventh Day — The Forearm

The Hawk is the forearm, and that which is the spine ....... *
The Crow is the dark elbow — the Crow is fatigued ....... *
The walking Cock and the rising Peacock are there .. *
O you who speak the pure Tamil, declare this ..................... (16)
<note>The eleventh day is *Ekadashi*. The bird's "fatigue" refers to the specific state of the bird's power (one of the five states: Ruling, Eating, Walking, Sleeping, or Dying) on this specific day.</note>

### Twelfth Day — The Finger

The index finger is the Hawk, the whole finger is the chest ...... *
The sought-after finger is the Crow, the straight line of hair — the pin .... *
The ring finger is the Cock, and the front eyes are the Peacock ..... *
Know that I have spoken this clearly .......................... (17)
<note>The twelfth day is *Dvadashi*. At this stage of the lunar cycle, the life-force is localized in the extremities, specifically the fingers.</note>

### Thirteenth Day — The Neck

The Hawk is the strong neck, the Owl is the mouth ....... *
The Crow is the throat, and the two eyes — hard to say ... *
The Cock is the forehead, the dancing Peacock ............ *
Know this through the people of the ocean-girt world ............................ (18)
<note>The thirteenth day is *Trayodashi*. The energy moves upward toward the head.</note>

### Fourteenth Day — The Body

The Hawk is the body, the Owl is the back ............. *
The two hands are the Crow — behold! — O lady with thick hair ... *
The Cock is the navel, and the two good feet ............ *
Declare these to be the Peacock ................... (19)
<note>The fourteenth day is *Chaturdashi*, the day before the full or new moon.</note>

### Full Moon — The Hair

The hair is the Peacock, the Cock is the forehead ....... *
The ears, eyes, and nose are the Crow — O you who have sought this .. *

### The Birds of the Lunar Days

The Cock rules for four days, prominent in this world... *
The ruling Hawk rules for five days—listen, my friend... *
The Crow is for two days, and the Owl is for three... *
The feathered Peacock is for one day, so it is said. (21)
<note>This verse establishes the duration each bird "reigns" over the progression of the lunar month. In the *Panchapakshi* system, the dominant bird changes based on the moon's phase.</note>

### The Birds of the Star Constellations

The Hawk is associated with the day beginning with the first star... *
The Owl rules for six days without fault—without fail... *
The Cock is for five days starting from the *Uttaram* star... *
The Peacock is for the remaining ten days. (22)
<note>The 27 Nakshatras (lunar mansions) are divided among the five birds. The "first star" refers to *Ashwini*.</note>

->(This is also from the *Arutkanitha Pakshi Virutham*)<-
With five, the Hawk is one; the Owl is nine... *
The Crow is five without youth; the Cock is for those... *
The Peacock is six in the heart; counting from the first day... *
These are the twenty-seven stars that dwell within. (23)

->(This is also from the *Ullamuudaiya Chudamani Jyotidam*)<-
Starting from the Horse-star, the first five are the Hawk for three days... *
The surrounding stars are the Owl; the five before the Pure Face... *
Without fail, those are the Owl; the five starting from the Big Palm... *
Are the Cock; the six starting from the Ear-star are the Peacock. (24)
<term>Horse-star (Ashwini)</term> <note>original: "Pari". The first of the 27 Nakshatras.</note>
<term>Ear-star (Shravana)</term> <note>original: "Ona". The 22nd Nakshatra.</note>

### The Birds of the Zodiac: Waxing Moon Phase

Aries, Leo, and Scorpio are the Hawk; the Crow is for... *
Taurus and Libra are the Cock—difficult to find... *
Cancer and Virgo were seen as the Owl; Capricorn and Aquarius... *
See the Peacock declared for these. (25)
<note>The *Purva Paksha* refers to the waxing phase of the moon. Each of the twelve zodiac signs is assigned a "ruling bird" during this period.</note>

### The Birds of the Zodiac: Waning Moon Phase

Aries, Taurus, and Cancer are the Crow... *
Virgo and Leo are the Cock—listen, O lady... *
Sagittarius, Pisces, and Libra are the Owl... *
See where the Hawk fills its place. (26)
<note>The *Abara Paksha* (or *Apara Paksha*) is the waning phase of the moon. The bird assignments shift when the moon begins to fade.</note>

Aries along with Scorpio and Leo are the beloved Hawk... *
Great Cancer, Gemini, and the Virgin-sign are the Owl... *

# Chapter Nine

## The Mathematical Grammar of the Birds—The Hawk

->(In the *Nerisai-Venpa* meter)<-

The innocent Hawk is seen as three or as one...*
If spoken as one, it shall manifest—listen to the song...*
One among the five is the state of Kingship...*
Be clear through these certain words. (1)
<gloss>(Meaning): The Hawk's durations: Sleep—2, Eating—2, Walking—2, Death—7, Ruling—1. Total duration—6 <term>Nazhigais</term>.</gloss>
<note>A <term>Nazhigai</term> is a traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes. The "mathematical grammar" refers to how the 2.5-hour period (5 Nazhigais) of a bird's rule is subdivided into different activities like ruling, eating, or "death" (absolute inactivity).</note>

->**The Owl**<-

Separated from its mate, it performs its actions...*
Knowing its food, it eats at the proper time—through illness...*
They say the Owl's staff is sharp and weary...*
The heart belongs to the King of Owls. (2)
<gloss>(Meaning): The Owl's durations: Walking—2, Eating—2, Sleep—2, Death—7, Ruling—7. Total duration—6 Nazhigais.</gloss>

->**The Crow**<-

One for when it sleeps, and two for its pure walk...*
One for when it rules with excess—O women like vines...*
The small meal at its nest is one...*
They have spoken this for the body of the Crow. (3)

<summary>This page concludes the association of birds with Zodiac signs and begins Chapter 9, which provides the specific time-units (Nazhigais) for the five states of activity (Ruling, Eating, Walking, Sleeping, and Death/Inactivity) for the Hawk, Owl, and Crow.</summary>

<keywords>Panchapakshi Shastra, Agastya, Zodiac signs, Rashi, Hawk, Owl, Crow, Nazhigai, astrology, mathematical grammar</keywords>

<page-num>58</page-num>
<header>Panchapakshi Shastra</header>

(Meaning): The **Crow's** durations: Sleep—2, Death—1.5, Walking—0.5, Ruling—1, Eating—1. Total duration—6 <term>Nazhigais</term>.
<note>A <term>Nazhigai</term> is a traditional unit of time used in Indian astronomy, equivalent to 24 minutes. A 6-nazhigai block equals 2 hours and 24 minutes.</note>

### The Cock,

In a faultless one-and-a-half, the Cock sleeps . . . . . . . . . . . . . *
In another one-and-a-half, it washes its mouth (eats)—victory . . . . . . . . . *
It rules in one, and it perishes in one . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . *
Think of the remaining one where it walks . . . . . . . . . . . . . . (4)

(Meaning): The **Cock's** durations: Walking—1.5, Eating—1.5, Ruling—1, Death—1, Sleep—1. Total duration—6 Nazhigais.

### The Peacock,

Taking two-and-a-half as the duration . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . *
In that time, the Peacock walks—following that . . . . . . . . . . *
It rules in one, and stays in sleep in one . . . . . . . . . . . . . *
In one, it enters the grey state of death . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . (5)

(Meaning): The **Peacock's** durations: Walking—2.5, Eating—0.5, Ruling—1, Sleep—1, Death—1. Total duration—6 Nazhigais.

### Changing the Bird's Durations,

Do not think that all five birds sleep . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . *
In the same single auspicious hour—O women! . . . . . . . . . *
They eat within the twenty-four <term>Nazhigais</term> . . . . . . . . . . . *
So the elders have spoken to us. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . (6)
<note>The poet addresses "women" (*Vanjiyare*), a common poetic convention in Tamil didactic literature to imply the sweetness or accessibility of the knowledge.</note>

### The Divisions of the Birds
->(In the *Aruseer-kazhinedil-adiya-asiriya-viruttham* meter)<-
<note>A <term>Viruttham</term> is a type of formal Tamil poetic meter. This specific variety consists of four lines, each having six feet.</note>

The direction of the Hawk and the Dog join together, the direction of the Lion and all directions are spoken of; the direction of the Serpent, the Rat, and the great Rabbit—these eight directions are where the birds flourish. In silence, the subtle direction of the day is the primary life-force direction; O lady with speech as sweet as a lutist, those who describe the bird divisions will know the fruit of all labors. . . . . . . . . . . (7)
<note>This verse connects the "Five Bird" system with the *Ashta-Dikpalas* (Eight Directions) and symbolic animals, suggesting that the bird's power is influenced by geographical orientation.</note>

### The Years of the Major Periods (Mahadasha),

If the state is Eating or Walking, the period is twenty-five years; if it is Sleep or Death, it is fifteen. For the state of Ruling, it is twenty; adding these together, the Major Period (*Mahadasha*) is one hundred years. The sub-periods of the months, the subtle daily directions, and the life-force directions are all explained according to the almanac; those who do not know this are beneath the gods, but the wise elders know it all. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . *
<note>This section maps the bird's five states (Eating, Walking, Ruling, Sleep, Death) to a 100-year human life cycle. In Indian astrology, a *Mahadasha* is a major planetary period that governs a person's life.</note>

### Friendly Birds,

For the **Peacock** . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . the Cock and the Crow are friends.
For the **Crow** . . . . . . . . . . . the Peacock, the Cock, and the Hawk are friends.
For the **Hawk** . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . the Crow is a friend.

->Attraction of the Zodiac Signs<-

For **Aries** ........................................................................... **Leo** is the attraction
For **Taurus** .......................................................... **Libra** and **Cancer** are the attraction
For **Gemini** ........................................................................ **Virgo** is the attraction
For **Cancer** ................................................ **Sagittarius** and **Scorpio** are the attraction

->Directions of the Zodiac Signs<-

**Cancer** and **Leo**—South .......................................... **Virgo**—South-West
**Libra** and **Scorpio**—West ................................... **Sagittarius**—North-West
**Capricorn** and **Aquarius**—North .................................. **Pisces**—North-East

->Interaction of Quarters<-
<note>This section describes "Tharikkal," a technical calculation regarding how the four quarters (padas) of a zodiac sign interact with or "consume" one another.</note>

The first quarter of **Aries** consumes the last quarter in **Gemini**. The 2nd quarter consumes the 3rd quarter. The 3rd quarter consumes the 2nd quarter. The 4th quarter consumes the 1st quarter. In this same manner, the other zodiac signs also consume.

->Aspects of the Zodiac Signs<-

**Aries, Cancer, Libra, Capricorn**: These are <term>Sara Rasis</term> <note>Movable signs</note>—they gaze upon the 11th sign from them.
**Taurus, Scorpio, Aquarius, Leo**: These are <term>Thira Rasis</term> <note>Fixed signs</note>—they gaze upon the 9th sign from them.
**Sagittarius, Gemini, Virgo, Pisces**: These are <term>Ubaya Rasis</term> <note>Common or Dual signs</note>—they gaze upon the 7th sign from them.

In **Aries**, the 1st quarter has a quarter-gaze on the 4th quarter of **Aquarius**. The 2nd quarter has a half-gaze on the 3rd quarter. The 3rd quarter has a three-quarter gaze on the 2nd quarter. The 4th quarter has a full gaze on the 1st quarter. Know that all zodiac signs gaze in this manner.

->Calculating the Auspicious Rise<-

A sign rising by its small quarter lasts 22 <term>Nazhigai</term> <note>A traditional unit of time; 1 Nazhigai equals 24 minutes. 22 Nazhigai is approximately 8 hours and 48 minutes.</note> — for itself.

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the "Pancha Pakshi Shastra" (Science of the Five Birds). # Science of the Five Birds
[A]

For the bird, the second sign <note>original: "இராசி" (Rasi); one of the twelve signs of the zodiac.</note> covers for the remaining two and a half nazhigais <note>A "Nazhigai" is a traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes. There are 60 nazhigais in a day.</note>, and in this manner, the 12 signs and 30 nazhigais are said to come and cover from the left. Understand that the sign being questioned is the "left," and the sign rising at that moment is the "Ascendant" <note>original: "உதயம்" (Udayam); the rising sign.</note>. Know that the Serpent <note>Rahu/Ketu</note>, Venus, and Jupiter are exalted planets. Mercury and the Moon are stable planets. The Sun, Mars, and Saturn are called earthly planets. By these, understand whether a query was heard in an exalted state, a middle-ground state, or an earthly state, and determine other matters in this same way.

### Movable, Fixed, and Dual Planets

->Moon - Mars - Rahu - Ketu . . . Movable Planets <note>original: "சரக்கிரகங்கள்" (Sara-grahangal)</note><-
->Sun - Venus . . . . . . . . . Fixed Planets <note>original: "திரக்கிரகங்கள்" (Thira-grahangal)</note><-
->Mercury - Jupiter - Saturn . . . Dual Planets <note>original: "உபயக்கிரகங்களாம்" (Ubaya-grahangal)</note><-

### Planetary Aspects

All planets look at the 7th place from where they reside. Specifically, Saturn looks at the 3rd and 10th places; Mars looks at the 4th and 8th; Jupiter looks at the 5th and 9th; Rahu and Ketu look at the 3rd and 12th places. The 3rd and 10th places receive a quarter-aspect <note>original: "வ-பார்வை" (Va-parvai); short for 'Vakal' or 1/4 sight.</note>; the 4th and 8th places receive a half-aspect <note>original: "உ-பார்வை" (U-parvai); 1/2 sight.</note>; the 5th and 9th places receive a three-quarter aspect <note>original: "ஏ-பார்வை" (E-parvai); 3/4 sight.</note>. The 7th place and the place where the planet resides receive the full aspect. Furthermore, the Sun, Rahu, Ketu, Mars, and Saturn are cruel planets <note>Malefics</note>. Jupiter, Moon, Venus, and Mercury are auspicious planets <note>Benefics</note>. The Moon during the waning fortnight and Jupiter associated with Saturn are also considered sinful planets <note>Malefics</note>.

### Classifications of Signs and Planets

Pisces - Scorpio - Cancer - Jupiter - Moon: these are the Brahmin class.
Leo - Aries - Sagittarius - Mars - Sun: these are the... Kshatriya <note>Warrior</note> class.
Gemini - Libra - Aquarius - Mercury: these are the... Vaishya <note>Merchant</note> class.
Taurus - Virgo - Capricorn - Venus: these are the... Shudra <note>Laborer</note> class.
Saturn - Outcaste <note>original: "பஞ்சமன்" (Panchaman)</note> - Rahu - Ketu: these are called the Dalit/Pulayar class.

### Directions and Letters

East: Sun; Southeast: Mars; South: Jupiter; Southwest: Mercury; West: Venus; Northwest: Saturn; North: Moon; Northeast: Rahu and Ketu. On their respective days of the week, they are said to rise in these directions for 73 nazhigais. The "Yoni" and "Flag" <note>specific astrological points</note> last for 32 nazhigais, and the Deity for 42 nazhigais. Furthermore, the letters are: A-Aa for East; I-Ee for Southeast; U-Oo for South; E-Ae-O for Southwest; A-I-Ai for West; U-A-Au-O for Northwest; A-U-Au for North; Ah-Ahh-Ahhh <note>the sounds of the Aytham/Visarga</note> for Northeast. Thus they stand for 23 nazhigais. For the planets, the letters are: Ca-Nya for Mars; Ca-Nya for Venus; Ta-Na for Mercury; Tha-Na for Jupiter; Pa-Ma for Saturn; Ya-Ra-Ku...

<summary>This page details the classification of planets into movable, fixed, and dual categories, explains how planets "aspect" or influence different zodiac houses, and maps specific letters and planets to the eight cardinal and ordinal directions.</summary>
<keywords>Pancha Pakshi Shastra, planetary aspects, nazhigai, movable planets, fixed planets, dual planets, zodiac classification, directional astrology, Tamil alphabet phonetics</keywords>

# Science of the Five Birds
[Page 54]

The Sun: **A** - **Va**; the Moon: **Ma** - **La**; Rahu: **Ma** - **Na**; Ketu: <note>The letters associated with Ketu are missing or implied in the sequence.</note>—know that these letters belong to the planets and the directions in which they reside. Those who go to perform deeds during the hours governed by these letters will achieve the results associated with those planets.

->Planetary Asterisms<-

For these nine—Sun, Moon, Mars, Rahu, Jupiter, Saturn, Mercury, Ketu, and Venus—if one counts from the star Krittika <note>The third Nakshatra (lunar mansion) in Vedic astrology.</note> to Bharani <note>The second Nakshatra.</note>, there is one star for each planet. Observe the same measurement for the others as well.

->The Foot-Shadow known as Avasaya<-
<note>Original: "பாதச்சாயை" (Pada-chaya). This refers to a method of timekeeping where one measures their own shadow in "feet" or "finger-widths" to determine the time of day and its astrological influence.</note>
All that is lost shall be regained; the destruction of bonds.

| Month | Nazhigai for the first part | Nazhigai for the middle part | Nazhigai for the last part |

---

->(Foot-shadow Venpa Verse)<-
> Knowing the circle and remaining within the house . . . . . . . ❃
> Speak of it as the two nazhigais touched . . . . . . . . . . . . (1)

->Venpa Verse<-
<note>A "Venpa" is a specific form of classical Tamil poetry characterized by a strict rhythmic structure.</note>
> Folding the index finger, keep the Sun to one's right . . . . . . ❃
> Double the eight fingers—and join them . . . . . . . . . . . . ❃
> Observe the foot-shadow and speak the truth of what comes before . . . . ❃
> Knowing the family, speak of it every day . . . . . . . . . . . . . . (2)

# Chapter Ten

## Excerpts Transcribed from the Book of <term>Machamuni</term>
<note>Machamuni is one of the eighteen celebrated Tamil Siddhas, often associated with the preservation of esoteric knowledge regarding astrology and magic.</note>

### Rules for the Eight Acts
<note>Original: "Ashta Karma Vidhi." These refer to the eight supernatural actions in Indian occult traditions: Stambhana (immobilizing), Mohana (attracting), Vashikarana (subjugating), etc.</note>

->These are the birds of the waxing moon.<-
<note>Original: "Purva Paksha," the bright fortnight of the lunar cycle.</note>

Golden-yellow, black, slight-red, and white... *
The Peacock, Owl, Crow, Rooster, and Hawk are the five... *
<note>This verse assigns specific colors to the five symbolic birds used to calculate auspicious times during the first half of the month.</note>

---

->These are the birds of the waning moon.<-
<note>Original: "Apara Paksha," the dark fortnight of the lunar cycle.</note>

Silver, gold, red, green, and black are the five... *
The Hawk, Owl, Crow, Rooster, and Peacock are the five... *

---

->Compatibility for Performing Deeds<-

It is said that for the East, Friday, the star Uttiram <note>Uttaraphalguni</note>, and the eighth lunar day are a good combination. *
For the Southeast <note>Agni</note>, Tuesday, the star Revati, and the eighth lunar day are very good... *
For the South <note>Marali/Yama</note>, Sunday, the twelfth lunar day, and the star Puram <note>Purvaphalguni</note> are very good... *
For the Southwest <note>Niruthi</note>, Monday, the star Chithirai, Magam, Ayilyam <note>Ashlesha</note>, and the full moon are good... (3)

->More on the same.<-

For the West, Tuesday, the eighth lunar day of the waning moon, and the star Visakam are good.
For the Northwest <note>Vayu</note>, Wednesday, the stars Puram, Poosam <note>Pushya</note>, Ayilyam, and the fifth lunar day are good.
For the North, Thursday, the star Swati, and the fourteenth lunar day are very good.
For the Northeast <note>Isana</note>, Friday, the stars Puradam <note>Purvashadha</note> and Puram are good for removing defects... (4)

->The State of the Deeds.<-

The results for the East will be "Death" <note>Maranam; here meaning total failure or lack of result</note>; for the Southeast, the result is "Union."
For the South, the result is "Bondage"; for the Southwest, the result is "Sustenance."
For the West, the result is "Wealth/Possession"; for the Northwest, the result is "Sleep" <note>Inactivity or stagnation</note>.
For the North, the result is "Ruin"; for the Northeast, it is "Goodness." This is the general nature of the five birds... (5)

->Directional Worship.<-

For the East, use an abundance of red. For the Southeast, use black.
For the Southwest, use green. For the West, use the aforementioned black.
For the Northwest, use black as well. For the North, use golden-yellow.
For the Northeast, use white.
Applying the five colors in this manner, perform the worship of the birds... (6)

->Compatibility of the Birds.<-
->Between the Hawk and the Owl.<-

Among the birds, if the first bird is one, even if the second is sleeping...

The previous page concluded with alignments for the Southwest direction.</meta>

# Science of the Five Birds

Even if it is sleeping, or at its peak, the bird that remains supreme is the **Hawk**. <note>Original: "Valluru." In this system, the Hawk represents the highest state of power among the five birds.</note> The **Owl** <note>Original: "Aathai."</note> is not favorable if it is encountered in a state of decay; it is a heavy burden on one’s wisdom. If the Owl is in the state of walking or sleeping in the forest, it lacks strength and will not yield success. ..............................(7)

## For the Crow and the Rooster

The **Crow** <note>Original: "Kagam."</note> becomes strong upon its rising. The Hawk's power is insufficient when the Crow is at its peak of strength, even if the Crow is in the state of "Death" or "Ruling." <note>These refer to the five functional states or 'avathas' of the birds: Eating, Walking, Ruling, Sleeping, and Dying.</note> If the Crow is in the state of "Eating" in the early morning, it is highly powerful. If the **Rooster** <note>Original: "Kozhi."</note> attempts to leap during this time, it will be defeated by destiny. All other birds remain secondary; consider the Rooster and the Owl with great care. ..........................................(8)

## For the Peacock

Even if the **Peacock** <note>Original: "Mayil."</note> is in a state of "Death" or "Sleeping," it remains connected to the earth and cannot be defeated while "Walking." Listen to the decree of the world: even if a dead Peacock is scattered, the state of "Death" remains significant. The hand that holds this power will find that even kings may lose to them. Great goodness and wisdom can be gained. Other birds are insufficient compared to the Peacock; listen now to the great deeds these birds can perform. ..........................................(9)

## The Waxing Moon
<note>Original: "Purva Paksham," the period of the increasing moon.</note>

## Days, Birds, and Herbal Instructions

For the waxing moon, **Tuesday** and **Sunday** are under the authority of the **Hawk**. **Wednesday** and **Monday** belong to the authority of the **Owl**. **Thursday** is assigned to the **Crow**. To much delight, **Friday** belongs to the **Rooster**, and the noble **Saturday** is under the authority of the **Peacock**. If one attempts to take the essence of these birds when they are in conflict or not "Eating," the effort will be wasted. .........................................(10)

Listen to the instructions for harvesting <term>Mooligai</term> <note>Sacred medicinal or magical herbs used by Siddha practitioners.</note> on these days. For the first bird, use the *Panakkara* root. For the second bird, use the root cleansed by ritual water. We have instructed that for the fierce bird, one should use the *Aranai* root. For the Rooster, the *Sulai* root is required. For the Peacock, the *Nanjili* root should be joined daily. If these are plucked and used during the specific <term>Yama</term> <note>A traditional unit of time lasting approximately three hours.</note>, Oh Holy One, even death and great karmic deeds can be influenced. ...........................(11)

What is this karma? The states of "Eating" and "Ruling" are good for us; through them, our tasks will be accomplished and our foundations strengthened. As long as the bird is "Eating," our legal cases will be won, and if we go to war, we shall return with victory. Those who hold wealth may lose it, and kings may be defeated, but wealth will gather for the practitioner. Through these roots, all ailments can be healed. If the bird is in a state of "Sickness," the root will be effective. ...............................(12)

If you wish to conquer powerful enemies, observe the states of "Death," "Walking," and "Sleeping." In those moments, speak the name of the enemy and perform the ritual...

-> The Waning Moon — Notes <-

Listen to the signs for the cruel Waning Moon phase <term>Aparapaksha: the fortnight of the thinning moon</term>. On Thursdays, the Owl is in its "Eating" state. For the **Peacock**, the *Tai-velai* herb <note>Original: "Tai-velai." Traditionally used in Siddha medicine and alchemy; also known as White Gynandropsis.</note> is effective. For the **Rooster**, use the *Kuppaimeni* <note>The Indian Nettle (*Acalypha indica*), common in Tamil Nadu and used for skin and respiratory issues.</note>. If you perform your tasks with these herbs as I have described, observing the subtle techniques, you will see the results of your labor. As you arrange the vertices of the corner, establish the syllable **NA** <note>Original: "Nakaram."</note> alongside it. Observe how the previously mentioned types and the subsequently mentioned patterns align. Listen as I reveal this without any lack: one may then chant the auspicious "Namasi" <note>A shortened or specific form of the "Namah Shivaya" mantra used in ritual practices.</note> for success. ....................(17)

Praising the Supreme Being <term>Paraparam: The absolute divinity beyond all forms</term> who stood as the beginning; praising the feet of the master that grant the great liberation; I shall speak of the science of the Five Birds <note>Original: "Boothapatchi." I have revealed within this work all the occult secrets that were left unsaid in the three hundred verses. You shall conquer the world through the eight magical powers I have spoken into your ear. Now, listen to the superior states of the birds. Observe the seven letters starting with the letter 'A'—friend, listen to the West of those five letters. I shall speak of the eighteen letters—by removing their curses, the truth shall reveal itself. ....................(19)

"We know all there is to know about the birds," they say; "we have surely attained the eight great powers <term>Attasiddhi: A set of eight supernatural powers sought by yogis and siddhas</term>," they boast. ⁂ Let it be known that "Saram" <term>Saram: Literally "flow" or "movement," referring to the movement of air through the nostrils</term> is that which is referred to as air, mind, life-force, respiration, breath, *Prana* <term>Prana: The vital life-energy that sustains living beings</term>, soul, spirit, and life-breath.

# Shastra. 61

(Interpretation): Parvati asks, "Is there a way for those who do not know the rules of astrology—including the Moon, the Sun, the planets, the days, the fortnights, the <term>Karanas</term> <note>A Karana is half of a lunar day (*tithi*); there are 11 Karanas in Hindu astrology</note>, the <term>Yogas</term> <note>Specific alignments of the sun and moon</note>, and the days of the week—to understand the future through the thoughts of their own minds? If such a way exists, please graciously reveal it." 

Shankara <note>A name for Shiva meaning "the Giver of Joy"</note> replies, "O woman whose hands are adorned with beautiful bangles! Look with love upon this *Vindu Shastra* <note>original: *Vindu-chattiram*; a treatise on the vital essence or 'seed' of life</note>, which is the Science of the Breath. If you look into it, you shall realize your true self." (1)

The famous <term>Sutradhari</term> <note>Literally 'the string-holder' or director; here referring to the soul or the divine architect of the body</note> acts within the beauty of the lineage, which is like a long, four-stranded rope. Like a nose-rope threaded through a bull, the breath moves through the nostrils, bringing both the good and the evil 'poison.' The cruel 'Snake of Time' <note>A metaphor for Death or the relentless passage of time</note> consumes and releases us, leading to cycles of birth and death on this earth. Yet, all those who do not despise this Science of the Breath that we teach—they are the immortals; those who despise it are but mutes.

(Interpretation): This human body has inherited the traditions of the <term>Varnas</term> <note>The four social classes: Brahmin, Kshatriya, Vaishya, and Shudra</note> like a long, four-stranded rope. In this body, the breath (*charam*) stands in the pathway of the nose, acting exactly like a nose-rope used to control and move an ox. It feeds the individual the 'poison' of good and evil deeds. Under the influence of that poison, the cruel Snake of Time consumes and releases them, causing them to be born and die according to the fruits of their two-fold karma <note>Good and bad deeds</note>. However, whoever experiences this Science of the Breath through the guidance of a Guru without despising it, and follows that path, will collectively become as the gods. Those who are unable to know this are equivalent to mutes.

-> (He explains the rules for the seating positions to be used while observing the breath) <-

A mat of bamboo brings poverty; a seat of stone brings disease; sitting on bare earth brings sorrow; a wooden plank that is old or broken offers no benefit; mats made of grass or similar materials cause the destruction of one's lineage; a seat of green leaves causes mental agitation. In contrast, a seat of deer skin brings <term>Jnana</term> <note>Divine wisdom or spiritual knowledge</note>; a tiger skin brings great wealth; a seat of <term>Darbha</term> grass <note>A sacred grass used in Vedic rituals</note> leads to liberation; and a white cloth brings no harm but provides comfort. Sitting on such a refined seat, O bird-like woman <note>A poetic address to the Goddess</note>, speak... ... ... ... ... ...

(Interpretation): 
- Sitting on a bamboo mat: Poverty.
- Sitting on stone: Disease.
- Sitting on bare earth (without a seat): Sorrow.
- Sitting on a broken or split wooden plank/low stool: No benefit.
- Sitting on mats made of coarse grass: Destruction of the family/lineage.
- Sitting on green leaves: Mental agitation.
- Sitting on deer skin: Wisdom.
- Sitting on tiger skin: Wealth.
- Sitting on <term>Darbha</term> grass: Liberation (<term>Moksha</term>).
- Sitting on a white cloth: No harm.
- Sitting on a <term>Chitrasana</term> <note>A decorated or colorful seat</note> or a gem-studded rug: [Benefits implied].

# The Science of the Breath. 92

...and in these benefits, sitting in a posture that shines with goodness (one must observe the breath). I shall tell you more, listen, O woman as beautiful as a peacock! (3)

->(The method for observing the breath is stated here.)<-

Of the many postures mentioned, sit in the pure <term>Padmasana</term> <note>original: *pankaya-thanam*; the Lotus Position</note>. Removing all distress, making the mind one, performing the <term>Oddiyana Bandha</term> <note>An abdominal lock where the belly is drawn in and up</note> and <term>Pranayama</term> <note>Breath control</note>, look below the limitless navel where the letter resides. There, among the seventy-two thousand <term>Nadis</term> <note>Energy channels or "veins" in the subtle body</note>, ten are good; among those ten, three are primary. Along those three, the breath flows easily; O deer-eyed woman, observe that letter which brings great goodness. (4)

(Interpretation): There are many previously mentioned postures; among them, sit in the posture known as the Lotus Pose, which is without flaw. When observing, the mind may be troubled by memories; remove such troubles, allow no other thoughts, and make the mind one. Perform the breath control known as *Oddiyana Bandha*. Looking further, one will perceive the seventy-two thousand channels that exist in this body, starting from the letter located below the navel, which is the seat of infinite Grace. Among those channels, ten are considered good. Among those ten, three are the most excellent. It is through these three that the <term>Sara</term> <note>The flow of vital breath/energy</note> runs most easily. O woman with eyes like a doe, look upon that letter which yields the greatest benefit. Furthermore, for more details on this, see the 35th verse. (4)

O deer-eyed one! When this breath, called the movement of life, penetrates through the heart-lotus and leaves through the nostrils, and when it returns perfectly—completing its cycle—it happens twenty-one thousand six hundred times in a day. This is the nature of the supreme states known as <term>Mooladhara</term>, <term>Swadhisthana</term>, <term>Manipura</term>, <term>Anahata</term>, <term>Vishuddha</term>, and <term>Ajna</term> <note>The six primary chakras or energy centers in the body</note>. Will those who observe this Science of the Breath, which we teach without confusion, see the fruit of the Supreme Being?

(Interpretation): O woman with eyes like a doe! This breath, called the movement of life, passes through the heart-lotus without any harm and goes out through the nostrils. (4) (When it goes out by several inches) and returns properly, if one calculates, it moves twenty-one thousand six hundred times. This constitutes one day of 60 <term>Nazhigai</term> <note>A traditional Indian unit of time; 1 nazhigai equals 24 minutes, so 60 nazhigai equals a full 24-hour day</note>. All that movement is the nature of the supreme levels known as the Mooladhara, Swadhisthana, Manipura, Anahata, Vishuddha, and Ajna (the meaning is that the breath originates from there). If you ask how to see this, it is by observing this Science of the Breath, which we teach, without any mental confusion. Doing so...

<meta>catchword: Doing so...</meta>

# Science. 92

Those who understand the benefits arising from that one Reality, which shines as both the manifest and the unmanifest, will truly know. (5)

->The characteristics of the Moon, Sun, and Central Channel,<-
->and the results of travel are stated here.<-

If you observe, the left side is the Moon. Beside it, on the right, is the Sun. The central channel is the "Sea of the West," <note>original: *Me-kadalai*; a metaphorical reference to the central channel or <term>Sushumna</term></note> which is Fire. The Moon on the left is the nectar; it creates all things with clarity. When described, its color is black and its nature is a <term>Fixed Sign</term> <note>original: *thira rasi*; in astrology, these signs represent stability and endurance</note>. O woman with eyes like spears, listen with desire as we explain all of these in detail. ..................................................................... *

(Interpretation: If one investigates, that which is called <term>Idakalai</term> <note>the left energy channel</note> is the breath that flows through the left side; it is known as the <term>Chandra-kalai</term> (Moon-channel). That which is called <term>Pinkalai</term> <note>the right energy channel</note> is the breath that flows through the right side; it is called the <term>Suriya-kalai</term> (Sun-channel). When speaking of what is common to all, that which is called <term>Suzhumunai</term> <note>the central channel, <term>Sushumna</term></note> is the middle, and it is known as Fire. Furthermore, when describing the Moon, it is the nectar <note>original: *amudam*; the life-giving fluid or ambrosia</note> that produces all things with permanence. Its color is black, and in the zodiac, it is a Fixed Sign. O woman with eyes like spears, listen with interest as I clearly describe the remaining details.) Moreover, that central channel spreads and flows through two signs; it is also called "the Fullness." (6)

->(This is more of the same,)<-

When speaking of the Sun, he is all-pervading, of a pure white color, and a <term>Movable Sign</term> <note>original: *chara rasi*; signs associated with change and movement</note>. The fierce Fire, which destroys all actions when it speaks, is red in color and a <term>Dual Sign</term> <note>original: *upaya rasi*; signs that possess both fixed and movable qualities</note>. Know and understand well that the Moon is female, while the virtuous Sun is male. When traveling to a direction beyond a distance of twenty <term>Kadhams</term> <note>A traditional unit of distance, roughly 10 miles or 16 kilometers</note>, depart when the breath is on the left; to reach a nearby town, depart when it is on the right. ............................................................ *

(Interpretation: When describing the characteristics of the Sun—that Sun is all-pervading in the world and possesses a pure white color; in the zodiac, it is a Movable Sign. To speak of the Fire known as the central channel—it is a fierce red color that destroys all karmic actions, and in the zodiac, it is the combination of the two previously mentioned signs (a Dual Sign). If you look even more closely to understand, the Moon, called the left channel, is female. The Sun, called the right channel, is male. <insert>The central channel, called the Dual Sign, is neuter or of the third gender.</insert> Furthermore, when going to a distant country more than twenty *kadhams* away, let the Moon-channel be active; to reach a nearby...)

# Science of the Breath

I shall say even more, listen: if you depart when [the breath] is flowing and arrive in that land when the <term>Suriya-kalai</term> <note>the Sun-breath or right-nostril breathing</note> is flowing, [you shall succeed]. (7)

## Characteristics of the Moon: What may be performed when the Moon-breath is active.

> If you ask: wearing fine garments and gold jewelry, acquiring good-natured servants, 
> seeing water beneath the earth, domestic life, embarking on a journey, entering a new home, 
> selling, meeting kings, adhering to the truth, performing <term>shanti</term> <note>peace-making rituals or rites of atonement</note>, hunting, 
> installing a deity, curing fever or aversion, learning the arts, 
> sewing, and great acts of charity; 
> O one with a face like a blossoming lotus, these are all desirable... *

(Interpretation: If one asks which tasks may be performed when the <term>Chandra-kalai</term> <note>Moon-channel</note> is flowing through the left nostril: sending a messenger or going as a messenger oneself; wearing new clothes; putting on jewelry; entering into marriage <gloss>specifically, the tying of the <term>Thali</term></gloss>; acquiring a servant; digging wells, ponds, or lakes; buying a house or land for living; moving into a house; selling an object; meeting a king or those superior to oneself for the first time; speaking the truth; performing <term>shanti</term> rituals; installing an <term>Ishta Devata</term> <note>a personally chosen or beloved deity</note>; curing the breath; resolving aversions <gloss>reconciling with someone</gloss>; seeking education; accumulating wealth and assets; giving charity; and performing acts for the remission of sins. All of these will result in goodness. Understand that other tasks will yield only mediocre results. O woman with a face like a lotus flower that has bloomed this day, (this is the meaning). If you wish to know more of the actions for this, see verses 48 and 49.)
<meta>(8)</meta>

## Characteristics of the Sun: What may be performed when the Sun-breath is active.

> If you observe: receiving <term>upadesam</term> <note>spiritual instruction or initiation</note> on the right, learning the arts, service, 
> moving an army, agriculture, trade, theft, gambling, 
> arguing a legal case, riding elephants or horses, 
> applying radiant paints, singing music, 
> harsh words, enmity, removing gossip or demons, 
> mastering mantras, consuming medicine, sleeping, 
> bathing in structured waters, neutralizing deadly poisons, 
> and the yoga that subdues cruel diseases... *

(Interpretation: When investigating which things are fit to be done when the breath flows through the right nostril: receiving <term>upadesam</term> from a Guru; giving instruction to another; reciting or practicing the arts...

<summary>This page lists specific auspicious activities for the Moon (left) and Sun (right) breaths, ranging from domestic tasks like building a home to spiritual pursuits like mantra initiation. The Moon breath is generally linked to creative and stable actions, while the Sun breath is associated with more vigorous, intellectual, or combative endeavors.</summary>

<page-num>65</page-num>
<header>Science. 65</header>

...practicing the arts, teaching others, bowing before another, driving away an enemy's army, farming and cultivation, conducting trade, stealing, gambling, arguing a legal case that is difficult for anyone to resolve, riding upon and traveling by elephants, horses, or chariots, writing clear and shining letters, singing music, departing for another country, creating enmity, shaming someone, slandering <note>literally "telling tales" or "backbiting"</note>, exorcising ghosts, performing <term>Mantra-nishta</term> <note>disciplined meditation on a sacred sound</note>, taking medicine, consuming a meal, sleeping, bathing, performing rituals to avert deadly dangers, curing a cruel disease, and practicing <term>Stambhana Yoga</term> <note>the yogic power of "stiffening" or paralyzing an opponent's actions</note>—if one observes and experiences all these things during this time, they will result in benefit. 
— Thus it is said. For further details regarding the Sun and Moon breaths, refer to verse 46 and verse 48.

->He speaks of the results of the Sushumna, the Full Side, and the Empty Side.<-

> When the two breaths are equal, <term>Samadhi</term> is good, but no worldly thought will come to pass.
> If you seek to see, you shall not see. O one of great virtue, there is no worldly benefit; 
> only evil deeds may succeed. In the great abundance of the "Full Side," 
> legal cases, debates, and arguments grow great, and one may see kings.
> By placing the source of opposition on the "Empty Side," one wins; 
> all other attempts, O fish-eyed one, are but toil... *

(Interpretation:) When the breath flows as the <term>Sushumna</term> <note>original: *Suzhimunai*; the central channel where breath flows equally through both nostrils</note>, what tasks can be performed? It is beneficial to perform <term>Samadhi Yoga</term> <note>the state of resting quietly when the senses and tools of the mind have ceased their activity</note>. Not even one worldly thing you think of will succeed then. At that time, if someone asks if a certain object will be obtained, it will not be obtained. O woman of virtuous penance—when this <term>Sushumna</term> is active, no worldly benefit is available; only "dark" or destructive actions will take effect.

Setting this aside: (The side through which the <term>Saram</term> <note>the flow of breath</note> is moving is called the <term>Pooranam</term> or "Full Side"; the side where the breath does not flow is called the <term>Shooniyam</term> or "Empty Side"). 

If one wishes to meet those who are arguing a case, or those debating, or those fighting, or if one goes to see a King (or anyone superior to oneself), one should position these people on the "Empty Side" where the breath is not flowing. If the opponent is placed on the Empty Side, the one on the "Full Side" will win. Furthermore, if a man places such people on his non-breathing side, his words alone will prevail and he will be victorious. In whatever way you wish to focus and know things, you may know them thus—O woman with eyes like the Carp fish.
— Thus it is said.

## Remedies for Undertaking a Journey.

->He speaks of the remedy to be used if the alignment of the breath is incorrect.<-

> When attempting a journey, if the "Fullness" is on the left, take two steps before 
> moving to the right; if on the right, take three steps for the journey to succeed. 
> O woman with eyes like dark clouds! Your task will be completed. If you go 
> North or East when the breath is in the left, a singular enmity will arise 
> and you shall not return. If you go South or West when the breath is in the right, 
> you will suffer by fire or drown in water and meet death. O woman with a waist 
> as slender as a prayer-bead string, observe this well... ❃

(Interpretation:) Following what was said in the 19th verse—the West and South are the directions of the Moon <note>the left nostril breath</note>, while the North and East are the directions of the Sun <note>the right nostril breath</note>. This being the case, one who intends to travel must travel in the Moon's directions when the Moon-breath is flowing, and in the Sun's directions when the Sun-breath is flowing. Furthermore, as stated in the 7th verse, for journeys to places beyond twenty <term>Kadhams</term> <note>a traditional measure of distance; one kadham is approximately 10 miles or 16 kilometers</note>, one should start when the Moon-breath is active and arrive at the destination when the Sun-breath is active. 

If, at the time of departure, the breath is mismatched or has switched to the "wrong" side for that direction, the remedy <note>Tamil: <term>Upashantham</term></note> is as follows: If you must travel in a Sun-breath direction but the Moon-breath is currently flowing, you must draw that Moon-breath deep inside to make it "full," take two steps forward starting with that same side's foot, and then continue walking without stopping for a short distance. (This is the remedy). By doing this, the negative influence of the Moon-breath will be neutralized, and no harm will come. 

If you must travel in a Moon-breath direction but the Sun-breath is currently flowing, you must draw the Sun-breath deep inside, take three steps forward starting with that same side's foot, and then continue walking without stopping for a short distance. (This is the remedy for that). The power of the Sun-breath's ill effect will be neutralized, and no harm will come. 

O woman with eyes that dart like the <term>Sel</term> fish! The task you go for will be successful. Otherwise, if you travel North or East while the Moon-breath is flowing, you will face opposition from others at your destination and will not return. If you travel West or South when the Sun-breath is flowing, you will die either by fire or by water. O woman whose waist is so slender it seems to vanish, like the beauty of breasts tightened by a sash, believe this and observe—so it is said.

---

->(He speaks of the method for telling the good and evil results for a person.)<-

When someone comes to you seeking to know the outcome of a specific task, if they stand in front, to the left, or above you while the Moon-breath is flowing, it is good. If they stand to the right, below, or behind you while the Sun-breath is flowing, that is also favorable. If the number of letters in the words they speak is even, it belongs to the Moon...

<meta>This page transitions from the general rules of travel and directional breath alignment to the practical art of divination (prasna). It specifically addresses how a practitioner can predict the success or failure of a visitor's inquiry based on the visitor's physical position relative to the practitioner's active nostril.</meta>

<page-num>207</page-num>
<header>Science of Breath. Verse 7</header>

> If a visitor approaches and inquires while standing on the "Full" side of either 
> the Sun or Moon breath, all that is promised shall be successfully 
> attained. However, it will result in misfortune for those who speak from 
> the "Void" side... ❃

(Interpretation:) When someone approaches asking, "Will the task I desire turn out well or poorly?" observe the flow of breath as described in the previous verse. 

If the **Sun-breath** <note>Tamil: *Surya-kalai*; breath through the right nostril</note> is active and the visitor stands on the "Full" side <note>the right side, where the breath is flowing</note>, everything they spoke of will be achieved for them. If they do not stand there, but instead stand on the left side, or if the practitioner is positioned above while the visitor asks from below, or if they ask from directly behind, one should predict the outcome based on the specific power of the Sun-breath.

If the **Moon-breath** <note>Tamil: *Chandra-kalai*; breath through the left nostril</note> is active and the visitor inquires while standing on the "Full" side <note>the left side</note>, everything they mentioned will bear fruit. If they do not stand there, but instead stand on the right side, or directly in front, or if they are in an elevated position, one should predict the outcome based on the specific qualities of the Moon-breath.

Aside from this, if they stand and inquire from the "Void" side <note>Tamil: *Shunya-pakkam*; the side of the inactive nostril where the breath does not flow</note>, the matter they ask about will turn out poorly and will not succeed. 

Furthermore, count the letters of the very first word the visitor speaks. If the count is an **odd number**, attribute the qualities of the Sun to the outcome. If the count is an **even number**, attribute the qualities of the Moon. 

Regarding the mention of "Above" and "Below"—understand "Above" to refer to the upper floor of a house <note>literally "a terraced house"</note> and "Below" to refer to low-lying areas such as wells or pits.

As it is said in Verse 66, it is not necessary for all these signs to align perfectly during cloudy weather; the intention is that one may observe any one of these signs to provide an answer. This is further clarified by what follows.

Moreover, in a more detailed explanation: the direction and side of the Moon (even if it is "Full") is considered slightly mediocre <note>Tamil: *mathipam*; middling</note>. The direction and side of the Sun (even if it is "Void") is never useless; even a small sign there brings some benefit rather than being in vain. For the authority on this, refer to Verse 6 and Verse 7, where the Moon is called a **Fixed Sign** <term>Sthira-rasi</term> and the Sun is called a **Movable Sign** <term>Chara-rasi</term>. 

In these verses, if we attempt to provide a word-for-word commentary <note>Tamil: *Padavurai*</note> without losing any attributes, qualities, or modifiers, it becomes difficult to maintain a flowing, expanded explanation <note>Tamil: *Viriyurai*</note>. If we were to insist on forcing every single word into the flow, it would be like a wife who, during the intimacy of union with her husband, becomes overly concerned with "proper" conduct—worrying about her hands or feet touching him incorrectly or fearing a lapse in modesty. If she is so preoccupied, can the shared intention of the husband and wife be fulfilled smoothly and without obstacle? In the same way, here...

Because those without essence cannot grasp the inner meaning of these words, these instructions have been organized to suit the occasion—to be either accepted or refuted. <margin>(12)</margin>

This next section also concerns the same topic—specifically, the variations of the "Full" and "Void" states mentioned previously in verse 10.

> "I have lost something," or "I gave something away," "I am poisoned," 
> "One is dying in bed," or "I have caught a disease"—if the visitor 
> stands in the **Void** side, there will be no suffering and the issue 
> will be resolved. If they stand in the **Full** side, all that was 
> spoken will come to pass. O woman with a waist like a swaying 
> serpent! If the direction from which the visitor came is **Lunar**, 
> and the first word spoken is **Solar**, listen to what will happen 
> without any doubt... ❃

(Interpretation:) When a person approaches and says, "I have been ruined by such-and-such," or "I have lost such-and-such a thing"—or if they have been bitten by a venomous creature, or have consumed poison—or if they say, "A woman is lying in bed and her life is slipping away," or if they mention any such ominous signs of danger: If they stand on my **Void** side <note>Tamil: *shunya-pakkam*; the side of the inactive nostril where no breath flows</note>, you may say there is no cause for grief; the danger will be averted. 

However, if they stand on the **Full** side <note>Tamil: *poorna-pakkam*; the side of the active nostril</note> and speak, then whatever they said will manifest exactly: the lost item will not be recovered, the poison will not be neutralized, and the patient in bed will not recover. Furthermore, if someone speaks a greeting or a word while standing in the **Void**, it may at times prove to be a false or meaningless word; but a word spoken from the **Full** side will be the truth. 

Aside from this, O woman of great beauty! If you calculate the letters and the direction from which the visitor came—as explained in verse 12—and find that the direction corresponds to a **Lunar component** <note>Tamil: *Chandra-kura*; associated with the left breath/cooling</note> while the first word spoken corresponds to a **Solar component** <note>Tamil: *Surya-kura*; associated with the right breath/heating</note>, then even if the aforementioned calamities occur, they will only result in a small amount of distress and not a total disaster. Listen, for I shall tell you more. <margin>(13)</margin>

### Signs of Conception

> If the inquirer stands upon a "Living Soul," or holds 
> something living in their hands, or touches a living being, 
> a daughter will be born if they stand on the left; if on the right, 
> a son. If they stand on the **Void** side, it indicates a loss. 
> Look to the twin channels and the upward breath! 
> If the inquiry occurs when the **Void** becomes **Full**, 
> the mother's body may tremble, but the child shall live... ❃

(Interpretation:) When someone comes to inquire about a pregnancy: if the visitor is standing upon a "living soul" <note>this likely refers to standing on fertile ground or near another living creature</note>, or is holding something living in their hand, or is touching a living being, and if the side they stand on is the right side—specifically the **Full** side...

If the breath flows on the right side, say a boy will be born. If the breath is on the left and is **Full** <note>original: *poornamaanaal*; refers to the nostril where breath is actively flowing</note>, say a girl will be born. If it is not the **Full** side but the **Void** side <note>original: *shunya-pakkam*; the inactive nostril</note>, then regardless of what the person touches or even if they stand still: if the breath is on the right, it is a boy; if on the left, it is a girl—but that child will die. At that time, if the practitioner who observes the breath <note>original: *sarampaarkkiravan*</note> finds it in the **Central Channel** <note>original: *sushumna*; the middle state where breath flows through both nostrils equally</note>, say the child will be born neither as a male nor a female, but as an intersex person. If the person who comes to ask stands first on the right and speaks their purpose, and then moves to the left, say that the mother will suffer great pain in her womb before the child is eventually born. ### Signs of Pregnancy and Victory in Battle

> In the state of **Void**, any news of a birth is ill-omened. 
> In a battle between two, speak of the **Void**—
> The first person named will lose his strength.
> In the state of **Fullness**, the second person named will lose.
> When the **Central Channel** flows, the warriors will find peace.
> He who moves while the **Solar Breath** flows shall triumph,
> While he who opposes him during the **Lunar Breath** shall be defeated.
> If the **Central Channel** remains steady, no harm shall come to either side. ❃

(Interpretation:) In the previous verse, it was said that a child asked about during the **Void** state would die. Now, regarding victory in battle: if someone asks, "Between these two, who will win?"—if the visitor stands on the **Void** side when asking, the person whose name was mentioned first will lose. If the side is **Full**, the person whose name was mentioned second will lose. If at that time the practitioner’s breath is in the **Central Channel** <note>Tamil: *sushumna*</note>, the two parties will reconcile.

Furthermore, if a person is facing a battle, a debate, or a legal case: if the **Solar Breath** <note>Tamil: *surya-kalai*; breath in the right nostril</note> is active, he will win. The one for whom the **Lunar Breath** <note>Tamil: *chandra-kalai*; breath in the left nostril</note> is active will lose. If it is the **Central Channel**, they will reach a compromise. If a person finds the **Lunar Breath** active and wishes to win, they must follow the instructions in verse 11: draw the breath inward to make it **Full**, step forward with the foot corresponding to the active breath, and after taking five steps, face the opponent; they will then be victorious. (Verse 15)

> While Space scatters in all four directions—
> This is the knowledge of the breath for those on Earth. ❃

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions on Svara Shastra (the science of breath). Following the previous discussion on the gender of an unborn child and outcomes of conflict based on the "Full" and "Void" nostrils, this section focuses on identifying which of the Five Elements (Pancha Bhutas) is active within the breath. This is a diagnostic technique to understand the body's internal state and its interaction with the environment.</meta>

<page-num>88</page-num>
<header>Science of Breath</header>

When examining the different flows of breath and their practical effects, it is rare and difficult to perceive how the **Five Elements** <note>original: *panchabhuthiyangal*; the five fundamental elements: Earth, Water, Fire, Air, and Space</note> move within those channels. One must study them closely to experience them. If the breath <note>original: *saram*</note> emerging from the nose stays close to the septum <note>original: *mookku-thandu*; the bridge or central partition of the nose</note>, know it as the quality of **Earth** <term>Prithvi: the densest element associated with stability</term>. If it flows downward, know it as the quality of **Water** <term>Appu: the element associated with fluidity and coolness</term>. If it leaps upward, know it as the quality of **Fire** <term>Theyu: the element of heat and transformation</term>. If it blows toward the opposite side of the septum, know it as the quality of **Air** <term>Vayu: the element of movement and speed</term>. Aside from these four sides, if the breath moves mixed through all four directions, tell the people of the world that this is the quality of **Space** <term>Akash: the subtlest element, also called Ether</term>. This rule for the Five Elements is the same for both the right and left nostrils. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . (Verse 16)

### He describes the form, color, and essence of the breath

> The color is gold, its taste is sweet, and it has four corners;
> Its length is twelve finger-widths. Water is white—
> Shaped like a crescent moon, its taste is astringent;
> Its length is sixteen finger-widths. Fire is red,
> Taking the shape of a fine triangle, its nature is heat;
> Its length is eight finger-widths. Air is blue-black—
> Shining like a tiger, it is hexagonal; each side
> Flows for four finger-widths as it is told. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . *

(Interpretation): If you ask how else one can clearly identify the movement of the breath of the Five Elements: the **Earth** breath is golden, the **Water** breath is white, the **Fire** breath is red, and the **Air** breath is black. (Regarding the sensation or taste in the throat at that time: for **Earth**, the throat will feel sweet; for **Water**, it will be astringent; for **Fire**, it will be salty or burning; and for **Air**, it will be sour.) As for their shapes: **Earth** is an eight-sided figure <note>The verse mentions four corners, but the commentary clarifies it as an octagon or square</note>; **Water** is like a crescent moon; **Fire** is a triangle; and **Air** is a hexagon. Furthermore, regarding the distance the breath travels: know that **Earth** travels 12 **inches** <note>original: *angulam*; a unit of measure roughly the width of a thumb</note>, **Water** travels 16 inches, **Fire** travels 8 inches, and **Air** travels 6 inches. Additionally, because the word "essence" <note>original: *rasam*; can mean taste or mercury/essence</note> was used separately in the verse, we have categorized the tastes first. When explaining this to the people of the world, it may be difficult for them to distinguish these subtle differences, so he has provided four specific signs for each element—if one sign is not clear, it must be understood through one of the others. (Verse 17)

### Here he describes the good and bad qualities of actions performed while those elements are active.

The shape of **Space** <note>original: *vaan*; literally "sky" or "heaven"</note> is a circle; its length is just a tiny bit, a mere needle's point. Its color is like pure crystal... [The text continues to discuss the characteristics of actions taken under the influence of Air and Water].

<summary>This page explains how to identify which of the five elements is dominant in the breath by observing its direction, color, taste, shape, and the distance it travels from the nostrils. These physical markers serve as a diagnostic tool for the practitioner to understand the subtle energetic state of the body.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the detailed technical explanation of the Five Elements (Pancha Bhutas) within the Science of Breath (Svara Shastra). While the previous page defined how to identify the elements based on the direction of the breath's flow, this page expands on their sensory qualities (color, taste, shape) and their practical application in predicting the outcomes of combat and physical injury.</meta>

<page-num>91</page-num>
<header>Science</header>

The flow of Space is middling, it is said; like a crystal... the bow... the woman wearing many bangles... looking at the breath... saying "he is the one who watches," and bowing... who... *

(Interpretation) When the **Space** element <term>Akash: the element of ether or void</term> is active, its color is like a crystal. The taste in the throat is bitter. Its shape is circular, and its length is one inch. To speak of the qualities and virtues of the elements in this way: for **Earth** <term>Prithvi</term> and **Water** <term>Appu</term>, the outcome is excellent. For **Fire** <term>Theyu</term>, it is middling. For **Air** <term>Vayu</term> and **Space** <term>Akash</term>, the outcome is bad. 

O woman whose hands are adorned with bangles! To the one who examines the breath to provide a prediction, first offer **betel leaves and areca nut** <note>original: *tamboolam*; a traditional offering of respect and payment</note> and perform the proper hospitalities before asking your question. This being so, one must also follow the actions to be performed during the **Sun and Moon channels** <note>the right and left nostrils respectively</note> as mentioned previously in verses 7, 8, and 9. Refer also to verses 104 and 105. Even then, it is good for both the questioner and the practitioner if the one examining the breath partakes of betel leaves. (This is the meaning).

->He describes the signs for someone who asks about a battle.<-

The back is Earth, the legs are Water, the chest is the glowing Fire, the hands are the forest of Air, and the head is Space. Without moving, if one asks and the element is active in its named place without error, it will be a strike. On the side where the breath is **Full** <note>original: *purnam*; the nostril through which breath is currently flowing</note>, if one goes and strikes... even if the discus-bearing **Vishnu** <note>The Preserver god in Hinduism, known for his invincible chakra or discus</note> comes to intervene, the wound will still occur without fail when you mark it. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . *

(Interpretation) As stated in verse 15, without pointing at two people and without remaining on the **Void side** <note>original: *shunya*; the nostril that is currently closed or inactive</note>, if a person goes to war while on the **Full side**, what will happen to him? Say that he will return having been wounded by weapons. Specifically, if **Earth** is active at that time, say he will be struck on the back. If it is **Water**, on the leg. If **Fire**, on the chest. If **Air**, on the hand or arm. If it is **Space**, say he will be struck on the head. Even if Lord Vishnu himself comes to stop it, say he cannot escape without being struck. (Verse 16)

->He describes which weapon to take for each element and where to stand to fight.<-

Starting from the corner of the Air element... like a rope... the **Moon** <note>left nostril</note> below and the **Sun** <note>right nostril</note> above. Consider the West and South as the Moon's land; consider the bright Sun's land as the East and North. O woman with eyes like the carp fish! For **Earth**, the weapon is the sword; for the others, the bow; for **Air**, the heavy staff; and for **Space**, the weapon is a raised stone. Fighting in the prescribed land will bring victory. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . *
