(Interpretation) If one must engage in combat as mentioned above, one should visualize a straight line, as if a rope were held, extending from the **Northwest corner** <note>original: *Vayu-moolai*; the direction associated with the Air element</note> to the **Southeast corner** <note>original: *Akkini-moolai*; the direction associated with the Fire element</note>. Consider the West and South to be the **Moon’s portion** <note>original: *Chandra-kooru*; the cooling, receptive side</note> and the North and East to be the **Sun’s portion** <note>original: *Surya-kooru*; the heating, active side</note>. For the one who is fighting, if the **Sun channel** <term>Surya-kalai: breath through the right nostril</term> is active at that time, he should stand in the direction of the Sun and place the enemy in the direction of the Moon to engage in battle. While doing so (whether it be the Sun or Moon channel), the weapons to be wielded are as follows: "O woman with long eyes like the *sel* fish! If the **Earth** element <term>Prithvi</term> is active, use a knife. If **Water** <term>Appu</term>, use a sword. If **Fire** <term>Theyu</term>, use a bow. If **Air** <term>Vayu</term>, use a staff. If **Space** <term>Akash</term>, use a stone." By wielding the weapons corresponding to those elements and standing on the prescribed ground, one will achieve victory.

->He describes the signs of disease.<-

*If a man asks about a man's illness, if the question is from the right, it will heal. For women, if a woman asks from the left—even in sorrow, if the order is missed, the evil will not leave; it will return after many days. In the sign of Earth, days will pass; in Water, it returns quickly; in Fire, three days; if it exceeds that, it kills. In Air, two days; in the beautiful Space, if it is missed, they die on that day...*

(Interpretation) If a man comes to have the breath examined for an illness suffered by a man, and asks if this disease will be cured, the practitioner should observe: if the questioner stands on the right side of the person reading the breath, the illness will be resolved—there is no doubt. If a woman comes to inquire about a woman’s suffering, it will be cured if she stands on the practitioner’s left side—there is no doubt. (As stated in the thirteenth verse, it is best if the question is asked from the "empty" or inactive side of the breath). If this order is reversed—if a woman comes for a man or a man for a woman—the disease will be difficult to cure. Furthermore, if a person asks a question while standing on the right and then moves to stand on the left, or if a woman asks from the left and then moves to the right, the disease will be stubborn; however, it will slowly heal over many days. If the positioning is correct as described, the disease will depart. 

As for *when* it will be cured, look to the active element at that moment: if it is **Earth** <term>Prithvi</term>, it will take a few days. If it is **Water** <term>Appu</term>, it will be resolved quickly. If it is **Fire** <term>Akkini</term>, there will be liberation from the illness in three days; however, if the fever increases, it may kill. If it is **Air** <term>Vayu</term>, recovery comes in two days. If it is **Space** <term>Vanam/Akash</term>, the disease may appear to leave that very day, but if it does not, the patient will die the next day. (This is the meaning).

This section expands upon the nature described in the previous verse and the qualities of the elements <term>Bhutas: the five great elements of Earth, Water, Fire, Air, and Space</term> mentioned in the subsequent verses. As stated in verse 11, the practitioner who observes the breath <term>Sara: the flow of breath through the nostrils</term> must know that "this is the moment to go to a place" and "this is the moment to speak."

*If the breath flows through the central channel for five days, death is certain—this is the truth. The five elements are born and run their course; one must hear this from the mouth of a Guru. Beyond the specific benefits described, even the gods cannot understand the workings of this science through books alone. If one is summoned, go only when the breath is flourishing; listen well and speak when the Sun is high...*

(Interpretation: The specific qualities of the elements mentioned in the previous verse should not be ignored. As stated in verse 10, when the **central channel** <term>Sushumna: the breath flowing through both nostrils simultaneously, usually considered inauspicious for worldly matters</term> is active, what seems successful will fail, and what seems a failure may succeed. Similarly, if someone asks about a patient's illness while the central channel is active, that patient will die within five days. If they happen to escape death then, they will remain in danger until another calamity strikes; this is the truth.

Furthermore, as mentioned in verse 14, within the breath flowing on one side, there are five types of elements. Each of these five elements is further divided into five sub-parts. For example, within the **Earth element** <term>Prithvi</term>, there is:
1. Earth within Earth
2. Water within Earth
3. Fire within Earth
4. Air within Earth
5. Space within Earth

In this manner, the breath flows through these internal divisions. One must learn to recognize these and the fruits of this science through the **guidance of a teacher** <note>original: *Guru-mukhantara*; literally "through the mouth of the Guru"</note>. Even the gods, if they set aside the teacher and rely solely on scriptures or personal experience, cannot grasp this text. 

Furthermore, if someone comes to summon the practitioner, the practitioner should only set out while the **Sun channel** <term>Surya-kalai: breath flowing through the right nostril</term> is active. Whether speaking or giving a prediction, it must be done while the Sun channel is active; otherwise, it should not be done. If the breath is transitioning, the result will be mediocre. I shall tell you more, listen with an attentive mind.)

->He describes the nature of the elements as mentioned in verse 11.<-

*Listen to this: Earth and Water bring good traits; if one asks about pregnancy, it will be a boy. For the one who inquires, great profits and peace will follow. In the Fire element, one will conquer in battle. Space is inauspicious, and pregnancy under its influence will be difficult. If it is the Air element, the child will be a girl...*

...appears in the vast sky, if the practice of archery or yoga is performed when the breath is in a state of "perishing," it will fail and waste away.

The breath should appear on the side of the Sun or Moon during the first three days of the lunar fortnight; Mercury, Venus, and the Moon belong to the left, never failing; while the Sun, Mars, Saturn, and Jupiter belong to the right—O practitioner of clear mind, understand this well...

This cycle of three days repeats...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for "Swarodaya" (the science of breath). It transitions from the elemental compositions of breath to the specific timing of breath flow—either through the right nostril (Sun breath) or the left nostril (Moon breath)—based on the lunar calendar, days of the week, and stellar constellations.</meta>

<page-num>75</page-num>
<header>Science of Breath</header>

During the waxing and waning phases of the moon, on the **first** <term>Prathamai</term>, **second** <term>Dvitiyai</term>, and **third** <term>Tritiyai</term> lunar days, the **Sun breath** <term>Surya Kalai: breath through the right nostril</term> should arise and flow. Specifically, when five **units of time** <term>Nazhigai: a unit of 24 minutes</term> remain before dawn, this breath must arise and flow steadily without disturbance for the full five units. As for which breath should flow on which days of the week: on **Sunday, Tuesday, Saturday, and Thursday**, the Sun breath should flow. On **Monday, Wednesday, and Friday**, the **Moon breath** <term>Chandra Kalai: breath through the left nostril</term> should flow. To understand which **elemental flow** <term>Buthiyam</term> these breaths should arise in, one must refer to verse 45. If these elements flow with excessive intensity, the results are explained in verse 23; further details can be found in verse 33. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . (24)

<note>The following section groups several lines of verse and commentary regarding the constellations.</note>
Below, the text describes the specific times the breath should flow according to the **lunar mansions** <term>Nakshatras</term>. It explains that if the breath flows correctly on the days mentioned in the previous verse, there is no trouble; however, if it fails, specific misfortunes will occur.

> Understand that for nine days starting from **Purva Phalguni**, as well as the excellent **Rohini**, **Shatabhisha**, and **Uttara Bhadrapada**, the flow should be through the **Sun breath**. For the remaining days, it should be the **Moon breath**. If the fierce Sun breath fails <note>i.e., if the breath flows through the wrong nostril at dawn</note>, on Sunday it brings disease, on Saturday it brings poverty, and on Tuesday it brings death by the sword in battle. If it fails on Thursday, it brings ruin to kings; on Wednesday, the local authority is harmed; and on Monday, relatives suffer loss. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . *

(Interpretation) O Maiden, there is no need to be confused by the fact that we have given two different sets of rules—one for the **lunar day** <term>Tithi</term> and one for the **day of the week** <term>Vaaram</term>. Look to the details provided in the subsequent verses. Along with these two, one must also understand the third factor: the influence of the constellations. Discern which results are difficult and which are favorable by observing the movement of the breath. Note the timing required for the constellations: **Poorva Phalguni, Uttara Phalguni, Hasta, Chitra, Svati, Vishakha, Anuradha, Jyeshtha, Mula, Rohini, Shatabhisha, and Uttara Bhadrapada**. For these twelve, the Sun breath must flow. For the other fifteen constellations, the Moon breath must flow. Furthermore, if the breath fails to flow correctly on the designated days of the week:
- If it fails on **Sunday**: Disease.
- If it fails on **Saturday**: Poverty and loss of one’s possessions.
- If it fails on **Tuesday**: Conflict and death.
- If it fails on **Thursday**: Harm to one’s king <note>or patron/employer</note>.
- If it fails on **Wednesday**: Harm to the local authorities of that town.
- If it fails on **Monday**: Harm to one’s kinsmen.
(The terms "one's king" and "that town" refer to the person observing their own breath. For the results of **Friday**, see the next verse). Regarding the failure on Thursday: if the patron suffers, how can wealth come to the practitioner? Thus, the ruler of that person’s fortune is harmed...

...in that, it is said he becomes that very person and is given a position/place... (End of explanation)

In the following verses, the author describes the occurrences if the breath assigned to **Friday** <term>Velli: traditionally associated with the Moon breath/left nostril</term> fails, and further details what happens if the breath for specific **lunar days** <term>Tithi: a lunar day in the Hindu calendar</term> fails.

If the breath for the **unconquerable Venus** <term>Pukkar: a name for the planet Venus or Friday</term> fails, one will leave their town, suffer bodily pain, or experience the death of a spouse. In the **waxing phase** <term>Purva Paksha: the fortnight of the increasing moon</term>, if the first day fails, there is trouble; if the second, great conflict and loss of wealth; on the third, displacement occurs; on the fourth, relatives will suffer from disease; on the fifth, a mountain of arguments; on the sixth, kings will be ruined; and on the seventh, disease arrives................................................*

(Explanation) If the breath for Friday fails <note>In the context of Swarodaya, "failure" means the breath flows through the right nostril when it should be the left, or vice versa</note>, one will be forced to leave their place of residence or cross boundaries into other lands. Bodily exhaustion will occur, and one's wife may face death. <note>original: "சாவு" (Savu)</note> <note>The commentator notes that "death" here can also imply intense quarrels, friction, disease, or a total loss of compatibility between husband and wife.</note> Since the author firmly stated "death of the partner" and that distress will last until the end of life, one should seek goodness only through the increase of the **eight supernatural powers** <term>Ashta Siddhi</term> as mentioned in the subsequent verse. According to the text, these misfortunes may also befall the town's king or authorities. Furthermore, during the waxing moon:
*   Failure on the **first day** <term>Prathamai</term> brings some form of distress.
*   The **second day** <term>Dvitiyai</term> brings conflict.
*   The **third day** <term>Tritiyai</term> brings loss of property.
*   The **fourth day** <term>Chaturthi</term> brings displacement <note>This refers to all types of general loss or being "cast out."</note>.
*   The **fifth day** <term>Panchami</term> brings disease to one's kin.
*   The **sixth day** <term>Shasthi</term> brings distress to the people of that land and the king.
*   The **seventh day** <term>Saptami</term> brings debility to the king and the country. 

One should not remain idle thinking these do not affect oneself; the details in the verse explain that even if the trouble is for others, it may result in a "stain on one's reputation" or the sorrow of knowing one's own breath caused their suffering. (End of explanation) . . . (26)

->The following describes what happens if the lunar day fails and the nature of the Sun and Moon breaths.<-

If the **eighth day** <term>Ashtami</term> fails, sickness and death will occur. For the other phase not yet mentioned <term>Krishna Paksha: the waning moon</term>, follow this same order. When the Moon arises within the fierce Sun, or the fierce Sun arises within the Moon, one must realize this state through the guidance of a Guru. This was the method clarified when the **Lord of the Gods** <term>Devi-Piran: Lord Shiva</term> spoke to the **Goddess** <term>Umaiyal: Parvati</term> so she might understand and be enlightened...................................*

(Explanation) If the breath for the eighth day fails, disease and death will follow. While this was explained for the waxing moon, the same rules should be applied to the waning moon phase.

Does it not happen according to one’s **past karma** <term>Ool-vinai</term>?" One need not feel discouraged, thinking, "Is there a way to bring this under our control?" The practitioner of breath science should rise before the **break of dawn** <term>Arunodaya</term> as described in the previous verses. On **Sundays** and **Tuesdays**, face the South and take three steps forward; as mentioned in the 11th verse, make the breath "Full" <note>The breath is "Full" when it is flowing strongly through the nostril corresponding to that specific direction or planet</note> and place the leading foot first. On **Saturdays** and **Thursdays**, walk three steps to the West. On **Mondays** and **Fridays**, walk four steps to the North. On **Wednesdays**, walk two steps to the East. After walking in this manner, sit upon a divine seat <term>Asana</term>, meditate upon Me with songs of praise, and then arise. Wherever you go after this, or even if you remain there, none of the sufferings mentioned in the previous verses will occur.

<page-num>78</page-num>
<header>Science of Breath</header>

If one completes these practices for thirty-one weeks in this manner, they shall avoid death. Otherwise, if the **Pranayama** <term>breath control</term> continues without deviation toward the intended direction, even if the **Root-energy** <note>original: "moolam-ezhunthu," referring to the rising of the primal energy or Kundalini</note> does not rise, there will be no destruction of this person's life-force. They shall live as a **Jivanmukta** <term>one who is liberated while still alive</term>, and the **Eight Great Siddhis** <term>Ashta-siddhi: supernatural powers such as the ability to become infinitely small or large</term> will be attained by them. (26)

->The author now explains the method of divination through breath.<-

If a question is asked during **Rechaka** <term>exhalation</term> when the heart's intent is stirred, all those mentioned things will result in "fire" <note>meaning destruction or failure</note>. If asked during the excellent **Puraka** <term>inhalation</term>, all the things spoken of will become truth. If asked during the abundant **Kumbhaka** <term>breath retention</term>, it remains as "fire" <note>indicating a state of stasis or heat</note>. Do not seek answers during the undesirable **Rechaka**. If one observes the breath while passing waste or water, the enemies will certainly be destroyed without change..... *

(Explanation) If the person consulting the **Saram** <term>rhythmic breath</term> asks a question while the practitioner is performing **Rechaka** <term>exhalation</term>, the matter enquired about will not succeed. If asked during **Puraka** <term>inhalation</term>, what he says will be the truth. If it is during the time of **Kumbhaka** <term>breath retention</term>, it is a good sign and the matter will succeed. If the breath flows while discharging waste or urine, the enemies will be ruined. Further details on this must be learned directly from a **Guru** <term>spiritual teacher</term>; otherwise, it cannot be understood. (30)

->The author now explains the influence of Saturn and Jupiter.<-

During the waxing phase and the waning phase, if the **Moon-breath** <term>left nostril</term> and **Sun-breath** <term>right nostril</term> flow correctly for **Jupiter** <note>original: "Pon," meaning gold, a common name for the planet Jupiter</note>, great benefits will arise and the life-force will remain steady. All the matters mentioned thereafter will be successful. On the day of **Saturn** <term>Sani</term>, if the breath flows through the left or right nostrils without deviation during the day or night, O Lady of the Great Flower <note>an address to the Goddess Lakshmi or a poetic Muse</note>, we say this so that one may attain fame in this world... *

(Explanation) For **Jupiter** <term>Viyalan</term> during the **waxing moon** <term>Shukla Paksha</term>, the breath should flow through the **Moon** <term>left nostril</term>. For Jupiter during the **waning moon** <term>Krishna Paksha</term>, it should flow through the **Sun** <term>right nostril</term>. If this happens without fail, great fame will result, and one will live as a **Jivanmukta** <term>liberated soul</term> without exhaustion. Furthermore, on **Saturday** <term>Sani-kizhamai</term>, the Moon and Sun breaths must flow during the night and day respectively without switching. If this occurs, fame worthy of praise throughout the world will be attained. Also, one should observe the responses given for the breath cycles of the previous period. (31)

->The author now explains the method of divining a person's location.<-

If someone asks, "Where is this person?", the breath will reveal it: if it is the **Earth element** <term>Prithvi</term>, they are inside the house; if **Water** <term>Appu</term>, they are in the courtyard; if the powerful **Fire** <term>Teju</term>, they are nearby; if **Air** <term>Vayu</term>, they are at a distance; if **Ether** <term>Akasa</term>, they are resting. If they have left, they are on the earth; for water, they have returned; for fire, they are close; for air, they are in the open; O one with tresses like petals, if it is the **Ether** <term>Akasa</term> element, they will return within one **Nazhigai** <term>a traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes</term> without fail.

(Explanation) If someone arrives and asks, "Where is a certain person currently located?"—if at that moment the breath is in the **Earth** element <note>original: "Prithvi"</note>, they are inside the house. If it is in the **Water** element <note>original: "Appu"</note>, they are in the courtyard. If it is in the **Fire** element <note>original: "Theyu"</note>, they are within the village street itself. If it is in the **Air** element <note>original: "Vayu"</note>, they have crossed the boundary. If it is in the **Space** element <note>original: "Akaya"</note>, one should say they have either climbed a mountain or are asleep. Regarding a person who has traveled abroad, if asked: in the first element (Earth), they remain stationary in that town; in the second (Water), they are returning; in the third (Fire), the one who turned back is walking without stopping; in the fourth (Air), they have entered the town where the questioner resides; in the fifth (Space), one should say they will arrive home within one **Nazhigai** <term>a unit of time equivalent to 24 minutes</term>. ( )

->The author now explains what happens if this breath is dissolved and merged with the mind.<-

> When the breath merges with the mind in the Great Earth, Water, Fire, and Air, the life-force is perfected and death is conquered; the **Udanal** <note>likely referring to Udana Vayu, the upward-moving breath</note> stands still, and even at the time of sleep, there is no fear of the Lord of Death. Observe the sign: Earth takes one and a half **Nazhigai**; for Fire, count one and a quarter; for the dominant Air, it is three-quarters; and look, if the breath flows through the excellent **Nadi** <term>energy channel</term> for five **Nazhigai** in total, this is the path of moderation for the body... *

(Explanation) This breath, which alternates every one-and-a-half units between the left and right sides, if it becomes dissolved <term>Laya</term> within the body, there is no destruction for the **Prana** <term>life-force</term>. If it stands so dissolved, the **Antahkarana** <term>inner faculties</term> such as the mind have no work—the practitioner becomes motionless. Thereafter, there is no fear from followers of other sects, nor fear of **Yama** <term>the God of Death</term>. 

As previously mentioned, how long does the breath stay and flow on each side (left and right)? It is five **Nazhigai** <note>5 Nazhigai = 120 minutes or 2 hours</note>. If one asks why the proportions of the **Elements** <term>Pancha Bhuta</term> are not explained therein: in **Earth**, it flows for one and a half Nazhigai; in **Water**, one and a quarter; in **Fire**, one; in **Air**, three-quarters; and in **Space**, half a Nazhigai. Thus, the five **Nazhigai** comprised of the elements must flow perfectly on each side without faltering. If it is less or more than this, the body will suffer. One must seek the way for this breath to dissolve—if sought, the previously mentioned benefits will be attained. Otherwise, one must ensure the breath flows for five Nazhigai per **Nadi** <note>nostril/channel</note> without the elemental measures scattering. If that fails, only bodily suffering will come. — This is the meaning.

# Calculation for Knowing the Span of a Pure Life: The Determination of the Solstice Breath
## He explains the rules of the Solstices.

According to what was said by the one who grasped the truth, eat only once during the day preceding the solstice. On the next day, being pure of heart, observe the breath as it shifts from the left to the right. If the breath flows for five **Nazhigai** <term>a unit of time equivalent to 24 minutes; five Nazhigai is 2 hours</term>, one will live for a full hundred years. If the time is less, the life-force is diminished. According to the calculations of the Great One, for every **Ma** <term>a traditional fraction, here 1/20th of a Nazhigai</term> that the breath falls short, the lifespan is reduced accordingly; this is the truth...

(Explanation): For the practitioner of the Science of Breath <note>original: "Saram"</note> who wishes to know how many days have passed and how many remain in his life, the method is called **Ayanasara** <note>The breath during the solar solstices</note>. To observe this: on the first day of the month of **Thai** <note>mid-January, the start of the Northern Solstice or Uttarayana</note> and the first day of the month of **Aadi** <note>mid-July, the start of the Southern Solstice or Dakshinayana</note>, one must prepare. On the day before these dates, eat only one meal during the day, keep yourself pure, and allow the body to become light. This prevents the mind and the internal winds from becoming sluggish. 

On the morning of the first day of the month of **Thai**, five **Nazhigai** (two hours) before sunrise, observe the breath. Ignoring the lunar phase <note>tithi</note> and the star <note>nakshatra</note>, the breath should rise on the left side. If it flows steadily for exactly five **Nazhigai** as described in the primary texts, this is the sign to look for. Similarly, on the first day of the month of **Aadi**, one should observe the breath in the same manner. At that time, the breath should flow through the right side. 

If the breath flows for five full **Nazhigai** during these times, the practitioner—through the aid of his **Pranayama** <term>yogic breath control</term>—will live for a full hundred years. If the breath flows for less than five **Nazhigai**, one must calculate the remaining years. Divide the five **Nazhigai** into units of **Ma**. For every **Ma** (or combination thereof) that is missing from the full five **Nazhigai**, subtract the corresponding years from the century mark. However, if the breath is entirely reversed—flowing on the right in the month of **Thai** and on the left in the month of **Aadi**—know that the person has but a moment of life remaining. <margin>(104)</margin>

---

## He explains the Rules for Eating.

For the one who eats, while the breath is in the **Earth** element <note>Prithvi</note> or the **Moon** phase <note>Idakala/Left breath</note>, wash the hands and feet. Before eating, follow the proper conduct: first, consume foods like ghee, milk, and those with sour, salty, and pungent tastes. The stomach should be filled half with food (rice), one-quarter with pure water, and the remaining quarter must be left empty for the movement of air. After eating, clean the eyes, offer incense and flowers, and then enter a place where the darkness has been dispelled...

# Science of Breath. 83
<page-num>83</page-num>
<sig>[Ya]</sig>

(Meaning): As explained in the previous verse, the one who observes the breath <note>original: "Saram"</note> does not necessarily have to wait for the **Solar Breath** <term>Suriya-kalai; the breath flowing through the right nostril</term> to finish before eating. One may also eat when the **Lunar Breath** <term>Chandra-kalai; the breath flowing through the left nostril</term> is active. However, this must be done by choosing "hot" foods—those that are salty, bitter, or pungent—while limiting the consumption of watery or leafy vegetables. 

If, as prescribed, the Solar breath is active, one may eat foods like ghee, milk, or items that are sour, sweet, or astringent. Leafy vegetables and drinks may also be consumed at that time. While these are generally not suitable during the Lunar breath, if they must be eaten then, one should wait until the Solar breath returns. Nevertheless, if due to urgency or intense desire one cannot wait for the Solar breath and continues to eat during the Lunar breath, the results will still be fruitful provided one eats with moderation. 

When eating in this manner, the stomach should only be half-filled with food. Of the remaining half, one quarter should be reserved for water and the final quarter for the movement of air <note>This is a classic Ayurvedic guideline for digestion</note>. By maintaining this balance, one should then perform the union of the **Ten Channels** <term>Dasa-nadis; the primary energy pathways of the body</term> through the practice of **Pranayama** <term>yogic breath control</term>. With a focused and one-pointed mind <note>original: "Ekagra-chitta"</note>, one must then observe the breath. (53)

->He describes the method for bringing the breath under one’s control,<-
->ensuring it does not slip away or diminish.<-

> To those who know the calculation of the breath, nothing is evil; 
> Everything they intend shall be fulfilled. 
> To those who truly observe, the Great Earth, Water, 
> Fire, Air, and Space are revealed in their proper order. 
> O lady of milk-sweet speech! By binding and grasping the life-force, 
> Observing the nature of its flow,

(Meaning): O woman whose words are as sweet as milk! A practitioner might wonder: "Will my experience truly match what is written in this book? Will the rules of diet align perfectly with my life? If I eat too much or too little, will it cause disease? Will success not come easily?" 

If such doubts arise, one should remember the saying: "He who watches the breath watches the Supreme." For the one who observes the breath according to the correct calculations, no endeavor will ever fail. To those who see such a practitioner, all their intended tasks will be completed. 

How is this possible? It is because the practitioner understands the components of the **Five Elements** <term>Panchabhootas</term>: **Prithvi** <gloss>Earth</gloss>, **Appu** <gloss>Water</gloss>, **Theyu** <gloss>Fire</gloss>, **Vayu** <gloss>Air</gloss>, and **Akas** <gloss>Space/Ether</gloss>. Without losing their way, they use their conscious mind to grasp the sensations within the **Six Chakras** <term>Aadharas; energy centers of the body</term>. Beginning from the **Prana-nadi** <term>the primary life-breath channel</term>, which is the foremost of the ten channels, they perceive the three lights: the Moon, the Sun, and the Fire.

# Science of Breath.

...By observing these characteristics, it will become as clear and easy to understand as a calculation made in the palm of one's hand <note>original: "kaikkanippu"; a metaphor for something self-evident or easily calculated</note>.

### The Vision of the Shadow Being is described so that the practitioner of the Science of Breath may know good and evil omens, and the time of death.

On a level patch of ground, when the sky is clear and the sun is shining, at a time when your shadow is between five and ten feet long, stand facing your shadow. Let your arms hang down and gaze steadily without blinking at the shadow’s neck, hands, feet, or head. Then, look up at the sky without blinking. A form will appear there. If that form appears golden, one will obtain wealth. If it is white, there is no fear for one's life force and the lifespan will increase. If it is red, the lifespan will diminish and a loss will occur. If it is black, one will suffer. If, in that vision, the hands or feet do not appear, death will occur within six months. If the head is not seen and it appears as a headless torso, death will occur in three months. Furthermore, this can also be observed in the moonlight. This is called the Vision of the Shadow Being <term>Chaya Purusha Darshanam</term>. 

If one practices this Vision of the Shadow Being continuously for twelve years, the shadow that stands before him will begin to speak. Through its speech, one can obtain the Eight Great Occult Powers <term>Ashta-ma-siddhi; the classic yogic perfections such as becoming infinitely small or large</term>. Not only that, but after some days, that shadow will become a separate form that travels with him. If this practice is successful, the shadow will sit when he sits and rise when he rises. It will foretell the good and evil events that are to come. The Great Ones <note>the sages or ancestors</note> say that many such wonders will be produced. (67)

### The signs of death and the meaning of the bow-like vision.

He describes the signs of death and the result of the bow-like form. When the sky is clear, take water into the mouth and spray it out in front of you. If the water droplets catch the light and appear as a beautiful five-colored bow-shape <note>a man-made rainbow effect</note>, and if that bow-shape remains whole, the person will live for another year. If the bow is broken, he will die within that year. If the broken bow-shape appears as a bent or curved form, it indicates that the breath <note>original: "pranan"</note> is departing; listen carefully as I describe these omens.

(Meaning:) When the sky is clear and the sun is shining, your shadow will fall upon the ground. If it does not appear, death is certain. Furthermore, if a female form appears within those colors, he will die within six months. (38)

## He explains the rules for observing the Sun and Moon,

> Regarding the signs of the High Moon and the Diminished Moon, 
> Melt cow's ghee in a copper vessel and catch the moon's rays within it;
> If it appears white, red, golden, green, or black,
> Know the four results: life, prosperity, ruin, disease, or death.
> If the southern, western, northern, or eastern edges are diminished,
> The years and months are one, six, three, or one... <image-desc>ornamental flourish</image-desc>

(Meaning:) One should melt cow's ghee in a copper vessel and observe the reflection of either the Full Moon <term>Nirai Mathiyam</term> or, during the New Moon <term>Amavasya</term>, the Sun <note>original: "Kurai Mathiyam"; literally 'diminished moon,' used here to refer to the sun during the new moon phase</note>. The reflection of the Sun or Moon will appear in one of five colors: white, red, golden, green, or black. 

If it appears white, there is no fear for one's life force. If red, there will be prosperity. If golden, there will be ruin. If green, disease will come. If black, death will follow. 

Furthermore, regarding the timing of the Sun and Moon reflections:
- If the **southern** edge of the circle is diminished: one year of life remains.
- If the **western** edge is diminished: six months.
- If the **northern** edge is diminished: three months.
- If the **eastern** edge is diminished: one month.
- If there is a **hole or hollow** in the center: ten days. 

Know that "High Moon" refers to the Full Moon <term>Pournami</term> and "Diminished Moon" refers to the New Moon <term>Amavasya</term>. (39)

## He explains the signs of death, such as physical decay,

> Decay of the hands, forehead, and cheeks, with thick twitching;
> Six months, three months, or ten days, and life will end.
> Aside from this, the sound in the ears, 
> The light on the ground for seven days, the five senses...
> If one contemplates the pair of conchs, all karma will vanish.

(Meaning:) Twitching in the hands, legs, forehead, or cheeks indicates that the time of death has arrived. Specifically:
- If the **hand** twitches: one year remains.
- If the **foot** twitches: six months.
- If the **forehead** twitches: three months.
- If the **cheek** twitches: ten days.

# Science of Breath

Apart from what has been previously mentioned, if a rattling sound <note>resembling a crow's call</note> is heard in the throat, death will occur in seven days. If the eyesight grows dim, death is in five days. If a whistling or rushing sound is heard through the nose, death is in three days. If the tongue falters and speech becomes slurred, death is in two days. Knowing these signs, if one contemplates and praises the feet of the Lord, one will not suffer the karmic fruits of heaven or hell <note>original: "vinnpayan"; referring to the cycle of rebirth</note> but will instead merge into the Divine state. (Meaning) <margin>(39)</margin>

## He explains the benefits of Inhalation and Exhalation.

The breath that leaves is Shiva; the breath that enters is Shakti. If the breath is leaving when one speaks or acts, it is like poison; if it enters, all is gained. If the breath is held, poison is destroyed and does not spread. If a visitor arrives while the breath flows through the ear-gate <note>a poetic reference to the channel of the breath</note>, one should calculate the result. If the life-breath goes out, there is disease; if it enters, there is health. This is the truth we have spoken.

(Meaning:) When explaining the timing and benefits of exhalation <term>Rechaka</term> and inhalation <term>Puraka</term> <note>referred to here as Ushvasa and Nishvasa</note>: the breath that leaves the body is Shiva, and the breath that enters is Shakti <term>Shakti Saram</term>. Everything that happens while the breath is flowing out is considered "poison" <note>meaning unfavorable, yielding no results</note>. 

When a person says something or thinks of a task while the breath is flowing inward, that task will be accomplished. If it is thought of while the breath is flowing outward, it will not happen. If that breath is restrained within through breath retention <term>Kumbhaka</term>, even the effects of consumed poison will be neutralized. If the breath is properly contained, there is no need to fear death. 

To understand this in divination: when someone comes to ask for a sign, one should count the letters of the first word they speak. If the count is odd, it belongs to the portion of Shiva. If it is even, it belongs to the portion of Shakti. 

If someone asks, "Will this person go or will he come?" while the breath is leaving, the answer is that he will go but not return. If the breath is entering, he will return. If the breath is leaving and a person asks "Will he return?", or if the breath is entering and they ask "Will he go?", the conflicting signs mean the outcome will not be as desired. If the breath is leaving, he will only go; if entering, he will only come. Furthermore, if asked "Will this illness be cured?" while the breath is leaving, it will not be cured. Know that it will be cured only if the breath is entering. (Meaning)

## He explains the methods for Divination and for entering the Battlefield.

If a man speaks his request while the breath is "Full," but then it becomes "Empty," the outcome is bad. If he speaks while the life-force is indwelling, it is good; if it is outside, it is mediocre. If one enters the battlefield while the Moon breath flows, and is struck by the one in the Sun breath, the one in the Moon breath will lose. The Moon <term>Chandra</term> cannot win there. But if one goes out in the Sun breath and strikes in the heat of that breath, the body remains unharmed and he shall be the victor.

(Meaning:) If a person who comes to ask for an omen states his business first while the breath is "Full" <term>Puranam</term> <note>the active nostril</note> and then the breath shifts to "Empty" <term>Suniyam</term> <note>the inactive nostril</note>, that matter will end badly. If he stands first in the "Empty" side and then...

# Science of Breath
<page-num>85</page-num>
<header>Science of Breath</header>

If one is in the state of Fullness <term>Puraka</term> <note>inhalation or the breath being "full" in a channel</note>, the result is intermediate. If the breath is shifting between the two paths <note>the left and right nostrils</note> and a person arrives in a withered or weak state, they will not succeed. However, if they approach while the breath is in the "Full" side, you may declare that their mission will be completed; for most people, the task will result in success. When the Lunar breath <term>Chandra Kala</term> is active, if one goes out and encounters an enemy while the Solar breath <term>Surya Kala</term> begins to flow, the enemy—no matter how powerful—will be defeated. Conversely, if one sets out while the Moon breath is active and engages in battle while the Moon breath remains active, they will face defeat. Beyond this, if one sets out while the Sun breath is active and engages in battle while that same Sun breath flows, they will return victorious without a single strike falling upon their body.

->**He describes the method for winning a battle.**<-

Count the letters in the names of the two combatants. The one with the greater number of letters—associated with the auspicious vowels **A— I— U— E— O—** combined with **Aa— Ee— Uu— Ae— Oh—** <note>referring to the short and long vowel sounds of the Sanskrit/Tamil alphabet</note>—will find success. These are categorized as the Child <term>Balan</term>, the Youth <term>Kumaran</term>, the King <term>Arasan</term>, and the Elder <term>Vriddhan</term>. If you combine the first letter of the name with the corresponding vowel sound and place it in its respective station: the one whose name-letter resides in the first three qualities will win, while the one with an even number of letters or the "Elder" status will be defeated by the others.

(Meaning:) One must categorize the vowels **A, Aa, I, Ee, U, Uu, E, Ae, O, Oh** as follows: the first pair is the Child <term>Balan</term>, the second is the Youth <term>Kumaran</term>, the third is the King <term>Arasan</term>, and the remaining two pairs are the Elder <term>Vriddhan</term>. When examining a combatant's name, you must match the first letter’s sound to these vowels. According to these sounds, determine if they are the Child, Youth, King, or Elder. It should be said that the Child and Youth will defeat the King <note>This suggests the vitality of the earlier stages of life/vowels overcomes the established "King" stage</note>. The Elder will be defeated by all others. Specifically, those whose names are associated with **E— Ae— O— Oh—** (the "Elder" category) will be struck down by the others. (Meaning) <margin>(63)</margin>

->**He describes the method for identifying the signs of pregnancy.**<-

If a question regarding a child is asked when the breath is in the Dual state <term>Ubhaya</term> <note>when breath flows through both nostrils equally</note>, the child will be born healthy. If asked during the state of Fullness <term>Puraka</term>, it will be a boy. If asked during the Void <term>Sunya</term> <note>exhalation or the inactive nostril</note>, it will be a girl. For the woman with tresses like dark clouds, consider the timing: morning or evening. The planets—the Sun, Mercury, Saturn, the Moon, Mars, Jupiter, and Venus—along with the nodes <note>Rahu and Ketu, referred to as the Snake</note> and the elements must be aligned. If they stand in this prescribed path, great goodness will occur.

(Meaning:) In addition to the signs of pregnancy mentioned previously, if one asks a question while the dual-flow <term>Ubhaya</term> is occurring...
<sig>[2]</sig>

<summary>This page explains how to predict victory in battle and the gender of an unborn child by observing the flow of breath and the phonetic qualities of names. It categorizes names into life-stages (Child to Elder) to determine competitive advantage.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discourse on Svarodaya (the Science of Breath), specifically moving from combat and naming conventions to the divination of a child's gender, the elemental properties of the planets, and the spiritual practice required to master the breath.</meta>

# Science of Breath
<page-num>86</page-num>
<header>Science of Breath</header>

Regarding the children born, one may determine if they are twins or their gender by the side of the breath. The side of "Fullness" <term>Puraka</term> <note>the nostril through which breath is currently flowing</note> indicates a male, while the "Empty" side <term>Sunya</term> <note>the inactive nostril</note> indicates a female. However, keep in mind that the results mentioned in previous verses may vary. Furthermore, regarding the morning breath: if the breath flows through the left side <note>the lunar channel, Ida</note> for fourteen *kalas* <note>a unit of time based on breath duration</note>, and if the husband makes the effort to consciously draw that breath inward, his wife will come under his influence and will later look upon him with great desire. Some also say that this should be done by drawing the breath inward twelve times. 

Additionally, if you wish to know which areas the weekly breaths should arise in: Mercury belongs to Earth <term>Prithvi</term>; the Moon to Water <term>Appu</term>; Saturn and Mercury to Fire <term>Theyu</term>; Jupiter and Mars to Air <term>Vayu</term>; and Venus to Ether <term>Akasa</term>. If the breath flows correctly according to these planetary days at the time of sunrise, it will yield great benefits. — (64)

## He describes the origin of these elements.

> Earth, Water, Air, the Life-force, Fire, and Ether—when these are spoken of,
> Speak also of the qualities of the elements with praise; 
> The incomparable Sun is the movable <term>Chara</term> sign, the fresh white Moon is the fixed <term>Stira</term> sign,
> And the hidden, rising Sushumna is the dual <term>Ubhaya</term> sign;
> Sitting in the Lotus Posture, realize the state where the life-force bears fruit 
> By analyzing the breath's flow through the days. (65)

(Commentary): The root of Earth is Water; for Water, it is Air; for Air, it is the Soul <term>Jiva</term>—thus, one element acts as the foundation for another in succession. Although all these details are not explained exhaustively here, one cannot perceive the Sun channel <note>the movable sign</note>, the Moon channel <note>the fixed sign</note>, the Sushumna <note>the dual sign</note>, or their various effects without practicing Shiva Yoga <note>meditation focused on the divine consciousness</note> with devotion while seated in the Lotus Posture <term>Pankaya Vadhanam</term>. Through this, the breath will not wander, and it is the path to becoming a liberated soul <term>Jivanmukta</term>. Otherwise, know that life is wasted. — (65)

## He explains the system of the three channels.

> If you listen to the ancient stability and the states of life and death 
> Within the Earth, the Sun, the Moon, and the Sushumna, 
> Great wealth will come if the Sun prevails; some destruction occurs if the Moon prevails; 
> If the breath is in the Sushumna, there is neither decrease nor increase;
> Even if enemies come for war, they will not approach if the breath is right; 
> The radiant Sun breath shall prevail as we have said.

(Commentary): Look upon this Science of Breath <term>Svara Nool</term> with devotion. Among the three channels <term>Nadis</term> known as the Sun, the Moon, and the Sushumna, if one listens to the fruits of meditation regarding the three states—life, destruction, and stability...

<summary>This page explains how breath-flow determines a child's gender and marital harmony, lists planetary-elemental correspondences, and emphasizes the spiritual necessity of the Lotus posture for mastering the three channels (Nadis).</summary>
<keywords>Svarodaya, gender prediction, Five Elements, Nadis, Sun and Moon channels, Lotus Posture, Shiva Yoga, planetary breath</keywords>

--- Page 97 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructions on Svarodaya (the Science of Breath), moving from the elemental associations of the planets to the practical effects of the lunar and solar breaths on daily life, success, and specific activities based on the five elements.</meta>

# Science
<page-num>87</page-num>
<header>Treatise on Science</header>

If the Sun <note>the breath in the right nostril, Pingala</note> is active in its proper place, people will attain goodness and great wealth. If it is the Moon <note>the breath in the left nostril, Ida</note>, it leads to harm and a deficiency in education. If it is the central channel <term>Sushumna</term>, things remain as previously stated. One who is favored by the God of Gods <note>likely referring to Shiva, the lord of the breath</note> will surely achieve victory in all endeavors. If the lunar breath is overly dominant, one should be careful not to engage in transactions, as they may lead to debt.

> When the breath strikes the solar path and the lunar path overlaps it,
> Or if it moves at the upper edge, it indicates the loss of goods,
> Mental distress, or even death; this path leads to ruin.
> But if the Moon is active when one sets out, let out a long breath,
> Double the steps, and proceed—this will bring success.
> If the Sun is active, draw the breath inward, 
> Step firmly with the right foot, and proceed—this too is good.

(Meaning:) As previously mentioned, if the lunar breath <term>Chandra Kala</term> flows excessively, it brings harm to oneself and invites danger from enemies. One should not set out on important matters during such a time. However, if one must depart for a purpose when the lunar breath is active, one should exhale the breath <term>Saram</term> deeply and step forward with the foot on that same side first. Conversely, if one must depart when the solar breath <term>Surya Kala</term> is active, one should draw that breath inward and step forward with the right foot first. By proceeding in this manner, the purpose of the journey will be achieved. — (47)

### He describes the activities to be performed during the Lunar Breath.

> During the moving Moon, if the Earth element flows: build temples, walls, and houses.
> It is good for entering a home, departing, or planting trees. 
> During the Water element: it is excellent for digging fragrant wells, ponds, 
> Planting groves, plowing the earth, and performing marriages.
> During the Fire element: one may cure diseases. 
> During the Air element: ride horses, chariots, or camels.

(Meaning:) If the Earth element <term>Prithvi</term> is active during the lunar breath: building Shiva temples, walls, or houses; moving into a new home; departing on a journey; performing purification ceremonies; and planting trees are all auspicious. If it is the Water element <term>Appu</term>: digging ponds and wells, planting groves, plowing the land, and conducting marriages are beneficial. If it is the Fire element <term>Theyu</term>: it is a good time to treat and cure illnesses. If it is the Air element <term>Vayu</term>: it is auspicious for riding horses, chariots, or sailing ships.

->(Continuation of the same.)<-

During the Ether element <term>Akasa</term>, one should engage in prayer and meditation. For war, the middle of the path is best. When the sun is hot, the "Fullness" <term>Puraka</term> of the breath is always good. For various types of efforts across the eight directions, and for interactions between men and women, the solar breath's fullness is required. One who can control and direct this breath with mastery is truly powerful.

<note>The vocabulary below summarizes the technical terms used for the breath channels and the five elements.</note>
*   **Science of Breath** <gloss>Saranul</gloss>
*   **Lunar Breath / Left Channel** <gloss>Chandra Kala</gloss>
*   **Solar Breath / Right Channel** <gloss>Surya Kala</gloss>
*   **Central Channel** <gloss>Sushumna</gloss>
*   **Earth Element** <gloss>Prithvi</gloss>
*   **Water Element** <gloss>Appu</gloss>
*   **Fire Element** <gloss>Theyu</gloss>
*   **Air Element** <gloss>Vayu</gloss>
*   **Treatise/Science** <gloss>Sastram</gloss>
*   **The Flow of Breath** <gloss>Saram</gloss>

--- Page 98 ---
<meta>This page concludes the *Saranool Sastram* (Science of Breath). It emphasizes the spiritual rewards of this knowledge, asserting that the practice of breath-science transcends caste and leads to divine union.</meta>

# Science of Breath
<page-num>88</page-num>
<header>Science of Breath</header>

(Meaning:) When chanting mantras beginning with the element of Ether <term>Akasa</term>, one should act with the focused intent of a drawn bow; this principle applies to the solar breath <note>the right nostril, Surya Kala</note> as well. Furthermore, whether it concerns a specific direction or the flow of the lunar or solar breath, if a person stands on the "full side" <note>the side of the active nostril</note>, the intended task will be successful. One who can discern and prove the subtle meanings within this Science of Breath—including the complexities of direction, gender, "empty" and "full" states, and the significance of sacred letters—is a perfected practitioner; no one else can claim this title. (49)

-> (The Sambhavas) <-
<note>original: "Sambhariyor" – likely referring to practitioners of the Sambhava yoga tradition associated with Shiva.</note>

What does it matter if one is born into a lineage of those well-versed in the four Vedas <note>the Brahmins</note>? If they do not follow the path of this Science of Breath, they cannot be considered true seers. Those who understand the nature of all living and non-living things <term>Charachara</term> will be worshipped by everyone as a god upon this earth and will live to attain the highest state of union <term>Sayujyam</term> with the Supreme Being. Those who fail to see this truth will remain trapped in lowly births.

(Meaning:) Even if one is born into a high caste or a humble one, only those who understand the Science of Breath and follow its discipline are true Brahmins. By knowing the nature of all moving and unmoving things, they become like a deity on this earth, receiving the worship of all, and eventually merge with the Supreme Reality. Those who do not observe these signs will remain in a cycle of lesser births. (50)

Even if born into a lowly state, if one listens to this Science of Breath as taught by a true teacher <term>Satguru</term>, understands it deeply, and walks its path, there is no difference between that person and Myself <note>Shiva, the traditional narrator of this text</note>. Just as fire transforms everything it touches into its own fiery form, such a person becomes divine and does not die in vain. Truly, they shall sit upon my head like the crescent moon that I wear upon my matted locks.

(Meaning:) Even a person of humble birth who hears this science through a teacher, understands it, and lives by it, is identical to the Divine; there is no distinction. Just as fire has the nature of turning whatever it touches into fire, he becomes as We are. He shall not suffer a meaningless death. To emphasize this further: he will be honored like the crescent moon residing upon my matted hair <term>Jatapara</term>, sitting atop my very head. (51)

-> The Science of Breath — Concluded. <-
